Then, we have $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}J_{6}^{2}d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\int_{0}^{\alpha t}+\int_{\alpha t}^{\infty}\right)\left(|v_{\xi}^{C}|^{2}|v^{S}-v^{*}|^{2}+|v_{\xi}^{S}|^{2}|v^{C}-v^{*}|^{2}\right)\,d\xi$ Using Lemma 2.5 and Lemma 2.4 , we obtain $\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{\alpha t}+\int_{\alpha t}^{\infty}\right)|v_{\xi}^{C}|^{2}|v^{S}-v^{*}|^{2}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}^{2}\int_{0}^{\alpha t}e^{-C\delta_{S}|\xi-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta|}\,d\xi+C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}^{2}\int_{b^{\prime}t}^{\infty}e^{\frac{-C_{1}|\xi+\sigma_{-}t|^{2}}{1+t}}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}^{2}e^{-Ct},$ and similarly, (4.22) $\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{\alpha t}+\int_{\alpha t}^{\infty}\right)|v_{\xi}^{S}|^{2}|v^{C}-v^{*}|^{2}\,d\xi\leq\delta_{S}^{3}\delta_{C}^{2}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+\delta_{S}^{4}\delta_{C}^{2}e^{-Ct}.$ Thus, we obtain $\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}J_{6}^{2}\,d\xi\leq C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}^{2}e^{-Ct}.$ $\bullet$ (Proof of ( 4.16 )) From ( 4.15 ), we have $\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{6}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}J_{i}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{R}^{3/8}\varepsilon_{r}^{1/8}\delta_{BL}^{5/4}+(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{1/4}\delta_{BL})+C(\delta_{BL}^{2}\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{BL}\delta_{C}^{2})$ $\displaystyle+C(\delta_{BL}^{4}+\delta_{BL}^{2}\delta_{S}^{2}+\delta_{S}^{3}\delta_{BL})+C\left(\delta_{R}^{2}\varepsilon_{r}^{2}\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{R}^{2}\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{C}^{2}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{2}\right)$ $\displaystyle+C(\delta_{R}^{2}\varepsilon_{r}^{2}+\delta_{R}^{2}\delta_{S}^{2})+C\delta_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{2}+C(\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}).$ Using the smallness of $\delta_{BL},\delta_{C},\delta_{S}$ , and $\varepsilon_{r}$ , we obtain ( 4.16 ).
∎ Lemma 4.4 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exist positive constants $c$ and $C$ such that the following estimates hold: (4.23) $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|u^{C}_{\xi}|^{2}+|u^{C}_{\xi\xi}|^{2})|\bar{v}-v^{C}|^{2}d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\left(\delta_{BL}^{2}\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{R}^{2}\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}^{2}\right)e^{-ct}+C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{BL}^{2}\frac{1}{(1+\delta_{BL}t)^{2}}+C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}e^{-c\delta_{S}t}+C\delta_{C}^{2}\frac{\delta_{R}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{r}}e^{-c\varepsilon_{r}t},$ $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{BL}|^{2}|v^{R}_{\xi}|^{2}d\xi\leq C\varepsilon_{r}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta_{R}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta_{BL}^{3}\frac{1}{(1+t)^{\frac{7}{4}}},$ $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{BL}|^{2}|v^{S}_{\xi}|^{2}d\xi\leq C\delta_{BL}^{4}\delta_{S}^{3}e^{-c\delta_{S}t}+C\delta_{BL}^{3}\delta_{S}^{4}\frac{1}{(1+\delta_{BL}t)^{3}},$ $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{R}|^{2}|v^{S}_{\xi}|^{2}d\xi\leq C\varepsilon_{r}^{2}\delta_{R}^{2}\delta_{S}^{3}e^{-c\delta_{S}t}+C\delta_{S}^{4}\frac{\delta_{R}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{r}}e^{-c\varepsilon_{r}t},$ $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{BL}|^{2}\left(|v^{C}_{\xi}|^{2}+|u^{C}_{\xi}|^{2}\right)d\xi\leq C\delta_{BL}^{4}\delta_{C}^{2}e^{-ct}+C\delta_{BL}^{3}\delta_{C}^{2}\frac{1}{(1+\delta_{BL}t)^{3}},$ $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{R}|^{2}\left(|v^{C}_{\xi}|^{2}+|u^{C}_{\xi}|^{2}\right)d\xi\leq C\varepsilon_{r}^{2}\delta_{R}^{2}e^{-ct}+C\delta_{C}^{2}\frac{\delta_{R}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{r}}e^{-c\varepsilon_{r}t},$ $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{S}|^{2}\left(|v^{C}_{\xi}|^{2}+|u^{C}_{\xi}|^{2}\right)d\xi\leq C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}^{3}e^{-c\delta_{S}t}+C\delta_{C}^{2}\delta_{S}^{4}e^{-ct}.$ Consequently, we have (4.24) $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(u_{\xi}^{C},u_{\xi\xi}^{C})|^{2}|\bar{v}-v^{C}|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau+C\sum_{i\neq j}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(u_{i})_{\xi}|^{2}(|(v_{j})_{\xi}|+|(u_{j})_{\xi}|)^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{0}+C(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{2}+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{2}.$ Proof. Since the proof of Lemma 4.4 is similar to that of Lemma 4.2 , we omit it.
∎ 5. Zeroth Order Estimates In this section, we prove $L^{2}$ estimates, which is based on the method of $a$ -contraction with shifts. Lemma 5.1 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exist positive constants $c$ and $C$ such that $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|(U-\bar{U})(t,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{2}}+\delta_{S}\int_{0}^{T}|\dot{X}|^{2}\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,dt\\
&\quad\leq C\left\|U_{0}-\bar{U}(0,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{2}}+C(\varepsilon^{2}+\delta_{C})\int_{0}^{T}\left(D_{u_{2}}+D_{\theta_{2}}\right)\,dt+C(\varepsilon^{2}+\delta_{0})\int_{0}^{T}D_{v_{1}}\,dt\\
&\qquad+C\delta_{0}^{1/40}+Ce^{-c\delta_{S}\beta}.\end{aligned}$ 5.1. Relative Entropy Estimates First, recall that $\overline{U}$ is the superposition of the boundary layer, rarefaction, contact wave and shock wave: $\overline{U}(t,\xi)=U^{BL}(\xi)+U^{R}(t,\xi)+U^{C}(t,\xi)+U^{S}(\xi-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)-U_{*}-U_{m}-U^{*}.$ Then, from ( 1.5 ), ( 2.4 ), ( 2.6 ), and ( 1.18 ), $\overline{U}$ satisfies the following equations: (5.1) $\begin{cases}&\bar{v}_{t}-\sigma_{-}\bar{v}_{\xi}-\bar{u}_{\xi}=-\dot{X}(t)v^{S}_{\xi},\quad t>0,\quad\xi>0,\\
&\bar{u}_{t}-\sigma_{-}\bar{u}_{\xi}+\bar{p}_{\xi}=\left(\mu\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}+Q_{1}-\dot{X}(t)u_{\xi}^{S},\\
&\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\big(\bar{\theta}_{t}-\sigma_{-}\bar{\theta}_{\xi}\big)+\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi}=\left(\kappa\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}+\left(\mu\frac{(\bar{u}_{\xi})^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)+Q_{2}-\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\dot{X}(t)\theta^{S}_{\xi}.\end{cases}$ Here, $\bar{p}:=\frac{R\bar{\theta}}{\bar{v}}$ and (5.2) $Q_{i}:=Q_{i}^{I}+Q_{i}^{R}+Q_{i}^{C},\quad i=1,2,$ where $Q_{i}^{I}$ are error terms related to the wave interaction terms: (5.3) $\begin{split}&\begin{aligned} Q_{1}^{I}:=\big(\bar{p}-p^{BL}-p^{R}-p^{C}-p^{S})_{\xi}-\mu\left(\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{u^{BL}_{\xi}}{v^{BL}}-\frac{u^{R}_{\xi}}{v^{R}}-\frac{u^{C}_{\xi}}{v^{C}}-\frac{u^{S}_{\xi}}{v^{S}}\right)_{\xi},\end{aligned}\\
&\begin{aligned} Q_{2}^{I}&:=\Big(\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi}-p^{BL}u^{BL}_{\xi}-p^{R}u_{\xi}^{R}-p^{C}u_{\xi}^{C}-p^{S}_{\xi}u^{S}_{\xi})-\kappa\left(\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{\theta^{BL}_{\xi}}{v^{BL}}-\frac{\theta^{R}_{\xi}}{v^{R}}-\frac{\theta^{C}_{\xi}}{v^{C}}-\frac{\theta^{S}_{\xi}}{v^{S}}\right)_{\xi}\\
&\quad-\mu\left(\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{(u^{BL}_{\xi})^{2}}{v^{BL}}-\frac{(u^{R}_{\xi})^{2}}{v^{R}}-\frac{(u^{C}_{\xi})^{2}}{v^{C}}-\frac{(u^{S}_{\xi})^{2}}{v^{S}}\right)\end{aligned}\end{split}$ and $Q_{i}^{R}$ are error terms due to the rarefaction wave (5.4) $Q_{1}^{R}:=-\mu\left(\frac{u_{\xi}^{R}}{v^{R}}\right)_{\xi},\quad Q_{2}^{R}:=-\kappa\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}^{R}}{v^{R}}\right)_{\xi}-\mu\frac{(u_{\xi}^{R})^{2}}{v^{R}}.$ For $Q_{i}^{C}$ , recall from ( 2.9 ) that $Q_{1}^{C}=O(1)\delta_{C}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}e^{-2C_{1}\frac{(\xi+\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}},\quad Q_{2}^{C}=O(1)\delta_{C}(1+t)^{-2}e^{-2C_{1}\frac{(\xi+\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}}.$ To obtain the $L^{2}$ energy estimates, we use the method of $a$ -contraction theory with shifts, which is based on the relative entropy method. First of all, the (mathematical) entropy is given by $\eta:=-s=\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\ln\left(\frac{R}{A}\theta v^{\gamma-1}\right)=-R\ln v-\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\ln\theta+const.$ Then, the relative entropy is given by $\eta(U|\overline{U})=R\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)+\frac{(u-\bar{u})^{2}}{2\bar{\theta}},$ where $\Phi(z):=z-1-\ln z$ (see [ 29 ] ).
Thus, the relative entropy weighted by $\bar{\theta}$ is given by (5.5) $\displaystyle\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})=R\bar{\theta}\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\bar{\theta}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)+\frac{(u-\bar{u})^{2}}{2}.$ Lemma 5.2 . Let $U$ be a solution to ( 1.3 ) and let $\overline{U}$ be the superposed wave defined by ( 1.21 ). Let $a=a(t,\xi)$ be the weight function defined in ( 3.2 ). Then, we have (5.6) $\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a(t,\xi)\bar{\theta}(t,\xi)\eta(U(t,\xi)|\overline{U}(t,\xi))\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle=\dot{X}(t)Y(U)+\mathcal{J}^{H}(U)+\mathcal{J}^{P}(U)+\mathcal{J}^{I}(U)-\mathcal{J}^{good}(U)+\mathcal{J}^{bd}(U),$ where $\displaystyle Y(U)$ $\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-R(\theta^{S})_{\xi}\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)-\frac{R}{\gamma-1}(\theta^{S})_{\xi}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)\right]\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[(u^{S})_{\xi}(u-\bar{u})+\frac{(v^{S})_{\xi}\bar{p}}{\bar{v}}(v-\bar{v})+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\frac{(\theta^{S})_{\xi}}{\bar{\theta}}(\theta-\bar{\theta})\right]\,d\xi,$ (5.7) $\displaystyle\begin{split}&\begin{aligned} \mathcal{J}^{H}&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}(u-\bar{u})(p-\bar{p})\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\Big(\bar{\theta}_{t}-\sigma_{-}\bar{\theta}_{\xi}\Big)\left[R\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)\right]d\xi\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-\frac{\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi}}{v\bar{v}}(v-\bar{v})^{2}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\theta}(\theta-\bar{\theta})(p-\bar{p})+\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi}\frac{(\theta-\bar{\theta})^{2}}{\theta\bar{\theta}}\right]d\xi,\end{aligned}\\
&\begin{aligned} \mathcal{J}^{P}&:=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\left[\mu(u-\bar{u})\left(\frac{u_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)+\kappa\frac{\theta-\bar{\theta}}{\theta}\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)\right]\,d\xi\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-\mu\bar{u}_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)(u-\bar{u})_{\xi}+\kappa\frac{\theta-\bar{\theta}}{\theta^{2}}\theta_{\xi}\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)-\kappa\frac{(\theta-\bar{\theta})_{\xi}}{\theta}\bar{\theta}_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)\right.\\
&\phantom{+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\Big[\quad}\left.+\mu\frac{\theta-\bar{\theta}}{\theta}\left(\frac{u_{\xi}^{2}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)-\frac{(\theta-\bar{\theta})^{2}}{\theta\bar{\theta}}\left(\kappa\left(\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}+\mu\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)\right]\,d\xi\\
\end{aligned}\\
&\begin{aligned} \mathcal{J}^{I}:=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}aQ_{1}(u-\bar{u})-aQ_{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}-1\right)\,d\xi,\end{aligned}\end{split}$ and $\mathcal{J}^{good}:=\sigma\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[\frac{\mu}{v}|(u-\bar{u})_{\xi}|^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{v\theta}|(\theta-\bar{\theta})_{\xi}|^{2}\right]\,d\xi,$ and (5.8) $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{bd}$ $\displaystyle:=-\sigma_{-}a\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\Big|_{\xi=0}+a(u-\bar{u})(p-\bar{p})\Big|_{\xi=0}$ $\displaystyle\left.-a\left[\mu(u-\bar{u})\left(\frac{u_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)+\kappa\frac{\theta-\bar{\theta}}{\theta}\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)\right]\right|_{\xi=0}$ Remark 5.3 . Here, $\mathcal{J}^{H}$ and $\mathcal{J}^{P}$ represent the terms arising from the hyperbolic and parabolic parts of the NSF system, respectively, and $\mathcal{J}^{I}$ denotes the interaction terms between the boundary layer solution and the elementary waves. Remark 5.4 . From ( 3.2 ), we have $\sigma a_{\xi}>0$ . Therefore, the term $\sigma\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi,$ appearing in $\mathcal{J}^{good}$ is indeed a good term. Proof. Since the proof of Lemma 5.2 is almost the same as [ 29 , Lemma 4.3] or [ 20 , Lemma 4.4] , we omit the proof here.
∎ 5.2. Decompositions Here, we further decompose the right-hand side of ( 5.6 ) into good and bad terms. In what follows, the notation $U_{i}$ , $i=1,2,3,4$ introduced in ( 3.6 ) and the corresponding wave notation $U^{BL},U^{R},U^{C}$ , or $U^{S}$ will both be used. 5.2.1. Decomposition of $\mathcal{J}^{H}$ First, based on the identity (5.9) $(p-\bar{p})=\frac{R}{v}(\theta-\bar{\theta})-\frac{\bar{p}}{v}(v-\bar{v}),$ and $\|(v-\bar{v},u-\bar{u},\theta-\bar{\theta})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\varepsilon$ , the second line of $\mathcal{J}^{H}$ can be estimated as $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-\frac{\bar{p}}{v\bar{v}}(v-\bar{v})^{2}-\frac{1}{\theta}(\theta-\bar{\theta})(p-\bar{p})+\bar{p}\frac{(\theta-\bar{\theta})^{2}}{\theta\bar{\theta}}\right]\bar{u}_{\xi}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\bar{u}_{\xi}\left[-\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}^{2}}|\phi|^{2}+\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}\phi\chi\right]\,d\xi+C\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{u}_{\xi}|\big|\big(\phi,\chi\big)\big|^{2}\,d\xi.$ Now, $\mathcal{J}^{H}$ can be decomposed into following terms: (5.10) $\mathcal{J}^{H}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{J}^{H}_{i}+C\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{u}_{\xi}|\big|\big(\phi,\chi\big)\big|^{2}\,d\xi,$ where (5.11) $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{H}_{i}$ $\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\Big(\partial_{t}\theta_{i}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi}\theta_{i}\Big)\left[R\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)\right]\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}^{2}}|\phi|^{2}+\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}\phi\chi\right]\partial_{\xi}u_{i}\,d\xi,$ for $i=1,2,3$ , and $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{H}_{4}$ $\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}(u-\bar{u})(p-\bar{p})\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\Big(\partial_{t}\theta_{4}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}\Big)\left[R\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)\right]\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}^{2}}|\phi|^{2}+\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}\phi\chi\right]\partial_{\xi}u_{4}\,d\xi.$ $\bullet$ Decomposition of $\mathcal{J}^{H}_{1}$ : Using $|(\phi,\chi)|\leq C\varepsilon$ and by Taylor expansion of $\Phi$ at $z=1$ with $\Phi(1)=\Phi^{\prime}(1)=0,\Phi^{\prime\prime}(1)=1$ , we find that (5.12) $\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)=\frac{(v-\bar{v})^{2}}{2\bar{v}^{2}}+O\big(|v-\bar{v}|^{3}\big),\quad\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)=\frac{(\theta-\bar{\theta})^{2}}{2\bar{\theta}^{2}}+O\big(|\theta-\bar{\theta}|^{3}\big).$ In addition, we observe from ( 1.5 ) 3 and Lemma 2.1 that (5.13) $\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta_{1}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi}\theta_{1}$ $\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma-1}{R}\left(-p_{1}\partial_{\xi}u_{1}+\kappa\left(\frac{\partial_{\xi}\theta_{1}}{v_{1}}\right)_{\xi}+\mu\frac{(\partial_{\xi}u_{1})^{2}}{v_{1}}\right)$ $\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\gamma-1}{R}\bar{p}\partial_{\xi}u_{1}+C(|\bar{p}-p_{1}|+\delta_{BL})|\partial_{\xi}u_{1}|.$ Here, we use the notation $p_{1}=p(v_{1},\theta_{1})$ . Substituting ( 5.12 ) and ( 5.13 ) into ( 5.11 ), we obtain (5.14) $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{H}_{1}$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\bar{p}\left(-\frac{\gamma+1}{2\bar{v}^{2}}\phi^{2}+\frac{1}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}\phi\chi-\frac{1}{2\bar{\theta}^{2}}\chi^{2}\right)\partial_{\xi}u_{1}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\bar{p}-p_{1}|+\delta_{BL})|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\partial_{\xi}u_{1}\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a|(\phi,\chi)|^{3}\partial_{\xi}u_{1}\,d\xi.$ Using the inequality $\frac{1}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}\phi\chi\leq\frac{3}{4\bar{v}^{2}}\phi^{2}+\frac{1}{3\bar{\theta}^{2}}\chi^{2},$ together with $|(\phi,\chi)|\leq C\varepsilon$ , we obtain (5.15) $\mathcal{J}^{H}_{1}\leq-2C_{BL}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\partial_{\xi}u_{1}\,d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\bar{p}-p_{1}|+\delta_{BL}+\varepsilon)|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}u_{1}|\,d\xi,$ for some constant $C_{BL}>0$ .
Since $|p_{1}-\bar{p}|\leq C\big(|v_{1}-\bar{v}|+|\theta_{1}-\bar{\theta}|\big)\leq C(|v^{R}-v_{*}|+|\theta^{R}-\theta_{*}|)+C\delta_{0},$ we have $\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\bar{p}-p_{1}|+\delta_{BL})|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}u_{1}|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(v^{R}-v_{*},\theta^{R}-\theta_{*})||(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\partial_{\xi}u_{1}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\delta_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\partial_{\xi}u_{1}\,d\xi.$ Therefore, we obtain (5.16) $\mathcal{J}^{H}_{1}\leq-C_{BL}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\partial_{\xi}u_{1}\,d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(v^{R}-v_{*},\theta^{R}-\theta_{*})||(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}u_{1}|\,d\xi.$ $\bullet$ Decomposition of $\mathcal{J}^{H}_{2}$ : From ( 2.4 ) 3 , we have $\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta_{2}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi}\theta_{2}$ $\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma-1}{R}p_{2}\partial_{\xi}u_{2}=-\frac{\gamma-1}{R}\bar{p}\partial_{\xi}u_{2}-\frac{\gamma-1}{R}(p_{2}-\bar{p})\partial_{\xi}u_{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\gamma-1}{R}\bar{p}\partial_{\xi}u_{2}+C\varepsilon\partial_{\xi}u_{2},$ where $p_{2}:=p(v_{2},\theta_{2})$ . Following the same arguments in deriving ( 5.15 ), we obtain (5.17) $\mathcal{J}_{2}^{H}\leq-C_{R}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\partial_{\xi}u_{2}\,d\xi,$ for some constant $C_{R}>0$ . $\bullet$ Decomposition of $\mathcal{J}^{H}_{3}$ : Using ( 2.6 ) 3 and Lemma 2.4 , we have $|\partial_{t}\theta_{3}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi}\theta_{3}|=C\big(|\partial_{\xi}u_{3}|+|\partial_{\xi}\theta_{3}||\partial_{\xi}v_{3}|+|\partial_{\xi\xi}\theta_{3}|\big)\leq\frac{C\delta_{C}}{1+t}e^{-\frac{C_{1}(\xi+\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}}.$ Thus, we have (5.18) $\mathcal{J}^{H}_{3}\leq C\frac{\delta_{C}}{1+t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\frac{C_{1}(\xi+\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi.$ $\bullet$ Decomposition of $\mathcal{J}^{H}_{4}-\mathcal{J}^{good}$ : We first decompose $\mathcal{J}_{4}^{H}$ as $\mathcal{J}_{4}^{H}=\mathcal{J}_{41}^{H}+\mathcal{J}_{42}^{H},$ where $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{41}^{H}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}(u-\bar{u})(p-\bar{p})\,d\xi,$ $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{42}^{H}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\Big(\partial_{t}\theta_{4}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}\Big)\left[R\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)\right]\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\phantom{\mathcal{J}_{4,2}^{H}=}\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}^{2}}|\phi|^{2}+\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}\phi\chi\right]\partial_{\xi}u_{4}\,d\xi.$ Step I) Decomposition of $\mathcal{J}_{41}^{H}-\mathcal{J}^{good}$ Using ( 5.9 ) and ( 5.12 ), observe that (5.19) $\displaystyle(u-\bar{u})(p-\bar{p})-\sigma\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})$ $\displaystyle\leq\psi\left(\frac{R}{v}\chi-\frac{\bar{p}}{v}\phi\right)-\sigma\bar{\theta}\left(\frac{R}{2\bar{v}^{2}}\phi^{2}+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\frac{1}{2\bar{\theta}^{2}}\chi^{2}+\frac{\psi^{2}}{2}\right)+C|(\phi,\chi)|^{3}$ $\displaystyle\leq\psi\left(\frac{R}{\bar{v}}\chi-\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}}\phi\right)-\sigma\bar{\theta}\left(\frac{R}{2\bar{v}^{2}}\phi^{2}+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\frac{1}{2\bar{\theta}^{2}}\chi^{2}+\frac{\psi^{2}}{2}\right)+C|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{3}.$ Here, the last inequality follows from the fact that $\psi\left[\left(\frac{R}{v}\chi-\frac{\bar{p}}{v}\phi\right)-\left(\frac{R}{\bar{v}}\chi-\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}}\phi\right)\right]\leq C|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{3}.$ In order to apply the Poincaré-type inequality in Lemma 2.6 later, we freeze the variable coefficients in the quadratic form in ( 5.19 ) at the left end state $U^{*}$ of the viscous shock. To this end, using (5.20) $|(\bar{v}-v^{*},\bar{u}-u^{*},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{*})|\leq|(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{u}-u^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})|+C\delta_{S},$ we have $\displaystyle(u-\bar{u})(p-\bar{p})-\sigma\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq\psi\left(\frac{R}{v^{*}}\chi-\frac{p^{*}}{v^{*}}\phi\right)-\sigma^{*}\bar{\theta}\left(\frac{R}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\phi^{2}+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\frac{1}{2(\theta^{*})^{2}}\chi^{2}+\frac{\psi^{2}}{2}\right)$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\big(|(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})|+\delta_{S}\big)|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}+C|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{3}$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq-\frac{R\sigma^{*}\theta^{*}}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\left[\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right]^{2}-\frac{R\sigma^{*}}{2(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}\left[\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right]^{2}$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\big(|(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})|+\delta_{S}\big)|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}+C|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{3}.$ For the last inequality, we use the identity $\frac{R\theta^{*}}{2(v^{*})^{2}\sigma^{*}}+\frac{R(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{2(v^{*})^{2}\sigma^{*}}=\frac{(\sigma^{*})^{2}}{2\sigma^{*}}=\frac{\sigma^{*}}{2}.$ Therefore, we obtain (5.21) $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{H}_{41}-\mathcal{J}^{good}$ $\displaystyle\leq-G_{1}(U)-G_{2}(U)-D(U)+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})||(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{3}\,d\xi,$ where $\displaystyle G_{1}(U)=\frac{R\sigma^{*}\theta^{*}}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\left(\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right)^{2}d\xi,$ $\displaystyle G_{2}(U)=\frac{R\sigma^{*}}{2(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\left(\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right)^{2}d\xi,$ $\displaystyle D(U)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{\mu}{v}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{\kappa}{v\theta}|\chi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi=:D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}}.$ Step II) Decomposition of $\mathcal{J}_{42}$ Now, it remains to decompose $\mathcal{J}_{42}$ . Observe from ( 1.18 ) 3 that, $\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta_{4}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}$ $\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma-1}{R}\left(-p_{4}\partial_{\xi}u_{4}+\kappa\left(\frac{\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}}{v_{4}}\right)_{\xi}+\mu\frac{(\partial_{\xi}u_{4})^{2}}{v_{4}}\right)-\dot{X}\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}$ $\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\gamma-1}{R}\bar{p}\partial_{\xi}u_{4}+C(|\bar{p}-p_{4}|+\delta_{S})|\partial_{\xi}u_{4}|+|\dot{X}||\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}|,$ where $p_{4}=p(v_{4},\theta_{4})$ . Following the calculations in deriving ( 5.14 ), we find that $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{H}_{42}$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\bar{p}\left(-\frac{\gamma+1}{2\bar{v}^{2}}\phi^{2}+\frac{1}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}\phi\chi-\frac{1}{2\bar{\theta}^{2}}\chi^{2}\right)\partial_{\xi}u_{4}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\bar{p}-p_{4}|+\delta_{S})|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\partial_{\xi}u_{4}\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a|(\phi,\chi)|^{3}\partial_{\xi}u_{4}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+C|\dot{X}|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}|\,d\xi.$ For the last term of the above inequality, we use $\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}|\,d\xi\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}|\,d\xi\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{S}$ to have (5.22) $\displaystyle|\dot{X}|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}|^{2}+\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}|\,d\xi\right)^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}|^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}\theta_{4}|\,d\xi,$ where $M>0$ is defined in ( 3.1 ). Using ( 5.20 ), ( 5.22 ), $|\bar{p}-p_{4}|\leq C\big(|\bar{v}-v_{4}|+|\bar{\theta}-\theta_{4}|\big)$ and $|(\psi,\phi,\chi)|\leq C\varepsilon$ , we obtain (5.23) $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{H}_{42}$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}ap^{*}\left(-\frac{\gamma+1}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\phi^{2}+\frac{1}{v^{*}\theta^{*}}\phi\chi-\frac{1}{2(\theta^{*})^{2}}\chi^{2}\right)\partial_{\xi}u_{4}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|(\bar{v}-v_{4},\bar{\theta}-\theta_{4})|+\delta_{S}+\varepsilon)|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\partial_{\xi}u_{4}|\,d\xi+\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}|^{2}.$ 5.2.2. Decomposition of $Y$ We decompose $Y(U)$ into three terms by $Y(U):=Y_{1}(U)+Y_{2}(U)+Y_{3}(U),$ where (5.24) $\displaystyle Y_{1}(U)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[(u^{S})_{\xi}\psi+\frac{(v^{S})_{\xi}p^{*}}{v^{*}}\phi+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\frac{(\theta^{S})_{\xi}}{\theta^{*}}\chi\right]\,d\xi,$ $\displaystyle Y_{2}(U)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a(v^{S})_{\xi}\left(\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{p^{*}}{v^{*}}\right)\phi\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{R}{\gamma-1}(\theta^{S})_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{\bar{\theta}}-\frac{1}{\theta^{*}}\right)\chi\,d\xi,$ $\displaystyle Y_{3}(U)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-R(\theta^{S})_{\xi}\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)-\frac{R}{\gamma-1}(\theta^{S})_{\xi}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)\right]\,d\xi.$ Recall from ( 3.1 ) that the shift function $X(t)$ is defined as (5.25) $\dot{X}=-\frac{M}{\delta_{S}}Y_{1}.$ This implies $\dot{X}Y=-\frac{\delta_{S}}{M}|\dot{X}|^{2}+\dot{X}(Y_{2}+Y_{3})\leq-\frac{3}{4M}\delta_{S}|\dot{X}|^{2}+\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}\big(|Y_{2}|^{2}+|Y_{3}|^{2}\big).$ Combining ( 5.10 ), ( 5.16 ), ( 5.17 ), ( 5.18 ), ( 5.21 ), ( 5.23 ), and ( 5.24 ), together with the bound $|u^{S}_{\xi}|\leq C\delta_{S}^{1/2}|a_{\xi}|\leq C|a_{\xi}|$ ,
we obtain the following lemma. Lemma 5.5 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exist positive constants $C$ and $C_{2}$ such that the following holds. $\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\delta_{S}}{2M}|\dot{X}(t)|^{2}+CB^{C}(U)+B^{S}(U)+B^{rem}(U)+B^{I}(U)+\mathcal{J}^{bd}(U)$ $\displaystyle\quad-C_{2}(G^{BL}(U)+G^{R}(U))-G^{new}(U)-D(U),$ where $\begin{split}&\begin{aligned} &B^{C}(U)=\frac{\delta_{C}}{1+t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\frac{C_{1}(\xi+\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi,\\
&B^{S}(U)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}ap^{*}\left(-\frac{\gamma+1}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\phi^{2}+\frac{1}{v^{*}\theta^{*}}\phi\chi-\frac{1}{2(\theta^{*})^{2}}\chi^{2}\right)u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi,\end{aligned}\\
&\begin{aligned} B^{rem}(U)&=\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}|Y_{3}(U)|^{2}+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{3}\,d\xi+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi\\
&\quad+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|u_{\xi}^{S}|\,d\xi+C\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\bar{u}_{\xi}|\,d\xi+\mathcal{J}^{P}(U),\end{aligned}\\
&\begin{aligned} B^{I}(U)&=\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}|Y_{2}(U)|^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}aQ_{1}(u-\bar{u})\,d\xi-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}aQ_{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}-1\right)\,d\xi\\
&\quad+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big(|(v^{R}-v_{*},\theta^{R}-\theta_{*})||u^{BL}_{\xi}|+|(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})||a_{\xi}|\big)|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi,\end{aligned}\end{split}$ and (5.26) $\begin{split}&\begin{aligned} &G^{BL}(U)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}u^{BL}_{\xi}\,d\xi,\quad G^{R}(U)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}u^{R}_{\xi}\,d\xi,\end{aligned}\\
&\begin{aligned} G^{new}(U)&=\frac{R\sigma^{*}\theta^{*}}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\left(\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right)^{2}d\xi+\frac{R\sigma^{*}}{2(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\left(\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right)^{2}d\xi\\
&=G_{1}(U)+G_{2}(U),\end{aligned}\\
&\begin{aligned} D(U)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{\mu}{v}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{\kappa}{v\theta}|\chi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi=D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}},\end{aligned}\end{split}$ where $C_{1}>0$ is the constant defined in Lemma 2.4 , $\mathcal{J}^{P},\mathcal{J}^{bd}$ are given in ( 5.7 ) and ( 5.8 ), and $Y_{2},Y_{3}$ are defined in ( 5.24 ). Remark 5.1 . We note that, although $B^{C}(U)$ is defined in terms of $(\phi,\psi,\chi)$ for convenience, only the terms involving $\phi$ and $\chi$ arise in the decomposition, as seen in ( 5.18 ). 5.3. Leading Order Estimates Lemma 5.6 . There exists a constant $C_{S}>0$ such that $-\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}(t)|^{2}+B^{S}(U)-G^{new}(U)-\frac{3}{4}D(U)\leq-\frac{1}{4}G^{new}(U)-C_{S}G^{S}(U),$ where $G^{S}(U):=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}|u^{S}_{\xi}|\,d\xi.$ Proof. We introduce the following new variable $y$ : (5.27) $y(t,\xi):=\frac{u^{*}-u^{S}(\xi-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)}{\delta_{S}}.$ For a fixed $t>0$ , since $u^{S}$ is monotone decreasing, the map $\xi\mapsto y(t,\xi)$ is well-defined, and $\frac{dy}{d\xi}=-\frac{(u^{S})^{\prime}(\xi-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta}{\delta_{S}}>0,\quad\lim_{\xi\to+\infty}y(t,\xi)=1.$ Now, we define $y_{0}=y_{0}(t)$ as $y_{0}(t):=\lim_{\xi\to 0+}y(t,\xi)=\frac{u^{*}-u^{S}(-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)}{\delta_{S}}>0.$ Using $\sigma>0$ and $\sigma_{-}<0$ , together with ( 3.1 ) and ( 3.4 ), we obtain $|\dot{X}(t)|\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}\|(\phi,\psi,\chi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\rho^{S}_{\xi},u^{S}_{\xi},\theta^{S}_{\xi})|\,d\xi\leq C\varepsilon\leq\frac{\sigma-\sigma_{-}}{2},\quad\forall t<T.$ This implies $|X(t)|\leq\frac{(\sigma-\sigma_{-})}{2}t,\quad\forall t<T,$ and so (5.28) $-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta<-\frac{(\sigma-\sigma_{-})}{4}t-\beta<0,\quad\forall t<T.$ Therefore, by ( 2.5 ) and ( 5.28 ), we obtain $y_{0}(t)\leq Ce^{-C\delta_{S}|(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t+X(t)+\beta|}\leq Ce^{-C\delta_{S}t}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}\leq Ce^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}.$ Thus, we choose $\beta>0$ sufficiently large so that $0<y_{0}<\frac{1}{9}$ for all $t<T$ . We also observe that $a(t,\xi)=1+\sqrt{\delta_{S}}y,\quad\text{and}\quad\delta_{S}\frac{dy}{d\xi}=-u^{S}_{\xi}.$ $\bullet$ Estimate of $-\frac{M}{\delta_{S}}|\dot{X}|^{2}$ : First, using ( 2.12 ), we find that $\displaystyle\left|Y_{1}-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left(\psi-\frac{p^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\phi+\frac{p^{*}}{\theta^{*}\sigma^{*}}\chi\right)u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi\right|\leq C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{S}||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi.$ This implies that $\displaystyle\left|Y_{1}-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left(\psi-\frac{p^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\left(-\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right)+\frac{p^{*}}{\theta^{*}\sigma^{*}}\left(\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right)\right)u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi\right|$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{S}||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\left|\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right|+\left|\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right|\right)|u_{\xi}^{S}|\,d\xi.$ Using $1-\frac{p^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\left(-\frac{1}{\sigma^{*}}\right)+\frac{p^{*}}{\theta^{*}\sigma^{*}}\left(\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\right)=2$ together with $1\leq a\leq 1+C\sqrt{\delta_{S}}$ , we have $\displaystyle\left|Y_{1}+2\delta_{S}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi\,dy\right|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}^{1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{S}||\psi|\,d\xi+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{S}||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\left|\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right|+\left|\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right|\right)|u_{\xi}^{S}|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}^{3/2}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}|\psi|\,dy+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\left|\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right|+\left|\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right|\right)|u_{\xi}^{S}|\,d\xi.$ From ( 5.25 ), we obtain $\displaystyle\left|\dot{X}-2M\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi dy\right|\leq C\delta_{S}^{1/2}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}|\psi|dy+C\delta_{S}^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|a_{\xi}|\left(\left|\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right|+\left|\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right|\right)d\xi.$ This implies $\left(\left|2M\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi\,dy\right|-|\dot{X}|\right)^{2}\leq C\delta_{S}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}|\psi|^{2}dy+C\delta_{S}^{-1}G^{new}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|a_{\xi}|\,d\xi.$ Using the inequality $\frac{a^{2}}{2}-b^{2}\leq(a-b)^{2}$ for $a,b\in\mathbb{R}$ , we obtain (5.29) $-\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}|^{2}\leq-\frac{M}{2}\delta_{S}\left(\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi dy\right)^{2}+C\delta_{S}^{2}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}|\psi|^{2}dy+C\delta_{S}^{1/2}G^{new}.$ $\bullet$ Estimate of $B^{S}$ : First, note that $\displaystyle B^{S}(U)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{p^{*}\gamma}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\phi^{2}u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{p^{*}}{2}\left(\frac{\phi}{v^{*}}+\frac{\chi}{\theta^{*}}\right)^{2}u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi=:B^{S}_{1}(U)+B^{S}_{2}(U).$ Using the following algebraic inequalities for any $a,b,c\in\mathbb{R}$ : (5.30) $\displaystyle(a+b)^{2}\leq(1+\delta_{S}^{-1/4})a^{2}+(1+\delta_{S}^{1/4})b^{2},$ $\displaystyle(a+b+c)^{2}\leq(2+\delta_{S}^{-1/4})a^{2}+(2+\delta_{S}^{-1/4})b^{2}+(1+2\delta_{S}^{1/4})c^{2},$ we obtain $\displaystyle B_{1}^{S}(U)$ $\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{p^{*}\gamma}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\left(\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}+\left(-\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right)\right)^{2}u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq-(1+\delta_{S}^{-1/4})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{p^{*}\gamma}{2(v^{*})^{2}}\left(\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\right)^{2}u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi-(1+\delta_{S}^{1/4})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{p^{*}\gamma}{2(v^{*}\sigma^{*})^{2}}\psi^{2}u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}^{1/4}G_{1}+\delta_{S}(1+C\delta_{S}^{1/4})\frac{p^{*}\gamma}{2(v^{*}\sigma^{*})^{2}}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi^{2}\,dy,$ and $\displaystyle B_{2}^{S}(U)$ $\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{p^{*}}{2}\left(\left(\frac{\phi}{v^{*}}+\frac{\psi}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\right)+\left(\frac{\chi}{\theta^{*}}-\frac{\gamma-1}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right)+\left(\frac{\gamma-2}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right)\right)^{2}u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}^{1/4}G^{new}-(1+C\delta_{S}^{1/4})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{p^{*}(\gamma-2)^{2}}{2(v^{*}\sigma^{*})^{2}}\psi^{2}u_{\xi}^{S}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}^{1/4}G^{new}+\delta_{S}(1+C\delta_{S}^{1/4})\frac{p^{*}(\gamma-2)^{2}}{2(v^{*}\sigma^{*})^{2}}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi^{2}\,dy.$ Thus, we have (5.31) $\displaystyle B^{S}(U)$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}^{1/4}G^{new}+C\delta_{S}(1+C\delta_{S}^{1/4})\frac{p^{*}(\gamma^{2}-3\gamma+4)}{2(v^{*}\sigma^{*})^{2}}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi^{2}\,dy$ $\displaystyle=C\delta_{S}^{1/4}G^{new}+C\delta_{S}(1+C\delta_{S}^{1/4})\frac{(\gamma^{2}-3\gamma+4)}{2\gamma v^{*}}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi^{2}\,dy.$ $\bullet$ Estimates on diffusion terms: Recall that we have two diffusion terms $D_{u_{1}}:=\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{a}{v}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi,\quad D_{\theta_{1}}:=\kappa\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{a}{v\theta}|\chi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi.$ In order to use the Poincaré-type inequality, the diffusion coefficients should be written in terms the left-end state $U^{*}$ of the viscous shock. To this end, we use the following Lemma. Lemma 5.7 . (1) For any $\gamma>1$ , we have (5.32) $v^{R}-v_{m}\leq 0.$ (2) For any $1<\gamma\leq 2$ , (5.33) $v^{R}\theta^{R}-v_{m}\theta_{m}\leq 0$ holds. Proof. First, ( 5.32 ) directly follows from $v^{R}_{\xi}>0$ . Thus, it remains to prove the estimate ( 5.33 ). From ( 1.6 ), we have $v^{R}\theta^{R}=\frac{A}{R}(v^{R})^{2-\gamma}\exp\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{R}(s(v^{R},\theta^{R}))\right).$ Since the entropy $s(v^{R},\theta^{R})$ is constant along the rarefaction curve, i.e., $s(v^{R},\theta^{R})=s(v_{*},\theta_{*})=s(v_{m},\theta_{m})=:s^{r},$ it follows that $v^{R}\theta^{R}=\frac{A}{R}(v^{R})^{2-\gamma}\exp\left(\frac{\gamma-1}{R}s^{r}\right).$ Since $v^{R}$ is strictly increasing, we find that the monotonicity of $v^{R}\theta^{R}$ is determined by the exponent $2-\gamma$ . Therefore, $v^{R}\theta^{R}$ is strictly increasing when $1<\gamma<2$ , and it remains constant when $\gamma=2$ . This completes the proof of ( 5.33 ).
∎ Thanks to Lemma 5.7 , the coefficients for the diffusion term $D_{u_{1}}$ can be controlled as follows. First, note that $\frac{1}{v}=\frac{1}{v^{S}}+\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{\bar{v}}-\frac{1}{v^{S}}\right).$ For the last term of the right-hand side of the above equality, we use Lemma 5.7 to have $\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bar{v}}-\frac{1}{v^{S}}$ $\displaystyle=-\frac{\bar{v}-v^{S}}{\bar{v}v^{S}}=-\frac{v^{BL}-v_{*}+v^{R}-v_{m}+v^{C}-v^{*}}{\bar{v}v^{S}}$ $\displaystyle\quad\geq-\frac{v^{BL}-v_{*}+v^{C}-v^{*}}{\bar{v}v^{S}}\geq-C\delta_{0},$ together with $a\geq 1$ , we obtain (5.34) $D_{u_{1}}\geq\mu(1-C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{1}{v^{S}}|(u-\bar{u})_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi,$ Now, we estimate the diffusion term $D_{\theta_{1}}$ . Observe that $\frac{1}{v\theta}=\frac{1}{v^{S}\theta^{S}}+\left(\frac{1}{v\theta}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}-\frac{1}{v^{S}\theta^{S}}\right).$ For the last term of the right-hand side of the above equality, we have $\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}}-\frac{1}{v^{S}\theta^{S}}$ $\displaystyle=\frac{-\Big((v^{R}+\widetilde{v}-v_{m})(\theta^{R}+\widetilde{\theta}-\theta_{m})-v_{m}\theta_{m}+(v_{m}\theta_{m}-v^{S}\theta^{S})\Big)}{\bar{v}\bar{\theta}v^{S}\theta^{S}},$ where $\widetilde{U}$ denotes the superposition of the boundary layer, viscous contact wave, and viscous shock wave: $\widetilde{U}(t,\xi)=U^{BL}(\xi)+U^{C}(t,\xi)+U^{S}(\xi-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)-U_{*}-U^{*}.$ Then, using Lemma 5.7 , and the fact that $\widetilde{U}-U_{m}=O(\delta_{0}),$ the numerator of the right-hand side of the above equality becomes $\displaystyle-\Big((v^{R}+\widetilde{v}-v_{m})(\theta^{R}+\widetilde{\theta}-\theta_{m})-v_{m}\theta_{m}+(v_{m}\theta_{m}-v^{S}\theta^{S})\Big)$ $\displaystyle\quad=-\Big((v^{R}\theta^{R}-v_{m}\theta_{m})+v^{R}(\widetilde{\theta}-\theta_{m})+\theta^{R}(\widetilde{v}-v_{m})+(\widetilde{v}-v_{m})(\widetilde{\theta}-\theta_{m})+(v_{m}\theta_{m}-v^{S}\theta^{S})\Big)$ $\displaystyle\quad\geq-\Big(v^{R}(\widetilde{\theta}-\theta_{m})+\theta^{R}(\widetilde{v}-v_{m})+(\widetilde{v}-v_{m})(\widetilde{\theta}-\theta_{m})+(v_{m}\theta_{m}-v^{S}\theta^{S})\Big)\geq-C\delta_{0}.$ Therefore, we obtain (5.35) $D_{\theta_{1}}\geq\kappa\big(1-C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{1}{v^{S}\theta^{S}}|(\theta-\bar{\theta})_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi.$ In order to apply the Poincaré-type inequality with respect to the variable $y$ , we need to estimate the Jacobian $\frac{dy}{d\xi}$ . This is provided by the following Lemma. Lemma 5.8 . There exists a positive constant $C>0$ such that $\left|\frac{\mu}{y(1-y)}\frac{dy}{d\xi}-\delta_{S}\frac{(\gamma+1)}{2}\frac{\mu R\gamma}{\mu R\gamma+\kappa(\gamma-1)^{2}}\right|\leq C\delta_{S}^{2}.$ Proof. From [ 29 , (4.59)] , we have (5.36) $\left|\frac{(v_{+}-v^{*})^{2}}{(v^{S}-v^{*})(v_{+}-v^{S})}\frac{\mu}{v^{S}}\frac{v^{S}_{\xi}}{v_{+}-v^{*}}-\frac{\gamma(\gamma+1)p^{*}}{2(v^{*})^{2}\sigma^{*}}\frac{\mu R\gamma}{\mu R\gamma+\kappa(\gamma-1)^{2}}(v_{+}-v^{*})\right|\leq C\delta_{S}^{2}.$ Integrating ( 1.18 ) 1 , we obtain (5.37) $-\sigma(v^{S}-v^{*})-(u^{S}-u^{*})=0\,\,\text{and }-\sigma(v^{S}-v_{+})-(u^{S}-u_{+})=0.$ Then using ( 1.18 ) 1 , ( 5.37 ), and ( 2.10 ) 1 , the left-hand side of ( 5.36 ) can be rewritten as $\text{l.h.s. of \eqref{est:Jac1}}=\left|\frac{1}{y(1-y)}\frac{\mu}{v^{S}}\frac{dy}{d\xi}-\frac{\gamma(\gamma+1)p^{*}}{2(v^{*})^{2}\sigma^{*}}\frac{\delta_{S}}{\sigma}\frac{\mu R\gamma}{\mu R\gamma+\kappa(\gamma-1)^{2}}\right|.$ Using $|v^{S}-v^{*}|+|\sigma-\sigma^{*}|\leq C\delta_{S}$ , together with the identity $\frac{\gamma(\gamma+1)p^{*}}{2(v^{*}\sigma^{*})^{2}}=\frac{(\gamma+1)}{2v^{*}},$ we then obtain the desired estimate.
∎ Using Lemma 5.8 in ( 5.34 ) and ( 5.35 ), we obtain $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} D_{u_{1}}&\geq\mu(1-C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\int_{y_{0}}^{1}|\psi_{y}|^{2}\left(\frac{dy}{d\xi}\right)\,dy\\
&\geq\frac{(\gamma+1)}{2v^{*}}\frac{\mu R\gamma}{\mu R\gamma+\kappa(\gamma-1)^{2}}(1-C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\delta_{S}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}y(1-y)|\psi_{y}|^{2}\,dy\\
&\geq\frac{(\gamma+1)}{2v^{*}}\frac{\mu R\gamma}{\mu R\gamma+\kappa(\gamma-1)^{2}}(1-C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\delta_{S}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}(y-y_{0})(1-y)|\psi_{y}|^{2}\,dy,\end{aligned}$ and likewise, we have $\displaystyle D_{\theta_{1}}\geq\frac{(\gamma+1)}{2v^{*}\theta^{*}}\frac{\kappa R\gamma}{\mu R\gamma+\kappa(\gamma-1)^{2}}(1-C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\delta_{S}\int_{y_{0}}^{1}(y-y_{0})(1-y)|\chi_{y}|^{2}\,dy.$ Using Poincaré-type inequality in Lemma 2.6 , we obtain $\displaystyle D$ $\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\gamma+1)}{v^{*}}\frac{\mu R\gamma}{\mu $\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(\gamma+1)}{v^{*}\theta^{*}}\frac{\kappa R\gamma}{\mu R\gamma+\kappa(\gamma-1)^{2}}(1-C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\delta_{S}\left[\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\chi^{2}\,dy-\frac{1}{1-y_{0}}\left(\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\chi\,dy\right)^{2}\right].$ From the inequality $(a+b)^{2}\geq(1-\delta_{S}^{-1/4})a^{2}+(1-\delta_{S}^{1/4})b^{2}$ , we obtain (5.38) $\displaystyle\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\chi^{2}\,dy$ $\displaystyle=\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\left(\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi+\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\right)^{2}\,dy$ inequality, we have $\displaystyle\Big(\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\chi dy\Big)^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}^{-1/4}(1-y_{0})\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\Big(\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\Big)^{2}dy+(1+\delta_{S}^{1/4})\Big(\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi dy\Big)^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}^{-3/4}G^{new}+(1+\delta_{S}^{1/4})\left(\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\right)^{2}\left(\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi\,dy\right)^{2}.$ Therefore, we finally obtain (5.39) $\displaystyle D$ $\displaystyle\geq\frac{\gamma+1}{v^{*}}\delta_{S}(1-C\delta_{S}^{1/4}-C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi^{2}\,dy$ $\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma+1}{(1-y_{0})v^{*}}\delta_{S}(1+C\delta_{S}^{1/4}+C(\varepsilon+\delta_{0}))\left(\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi\,dy\right)^{2}-C\delta_{S}^{1/4}G^{new}.$ $\bullet$ Proof of Lemma 5.6 : Combining ( 5.29 ), ( 5.31 ), and ( 5.39 ), and using $0<y_{0}<\frac{1}{9}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle-\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}(t)|^{2}+B^{S}-G^{new}-\frac{3}{4}D$ $\displaystyle\leq\delta_{S}\left(-\frac{M}{2}+\frac{7}{8}\frac{(\gamma+1)}{v^{*}}\right)\left(\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi dy\right)^{2}+\delta_{S}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}-3\gamma+4}{2\gamma v^{*}}-\frac{2}{3}\frac{\gamma+1}{v^{*}}\right)\int_{y_{0}}^{1}\psi^{2}dy-\frac{2}{3}G^{new}.$ Choosing $M=\frac{2(\gamma+1)}{v^{*}}$ and using the inequality constant $\widetilde{C}_{S}$ . Combining ( 5.40 ) and ( 5.41 ), we conclude that $-\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}(t)|^{2}+B^{S}-G^{new}-\frac{3}{4}D\leq-C_{S}G^{S}-\frac{1}{2}G^{new},$ for some positive constant $C_{S}$ . ∎ 5.4. Estimates of the Remainder Terms In what follows, we estimate the remaining bad terms in Lemma 5.5 . 5.4.1. Estimates of $B^{rem}(U)$ and $B^{C}(U)$ Lemma 5.9 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exists a positive constant $C$ such that the following estimate holds. $\displaystyle|B^{rem}(U)|$ $\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{50}+C\varepsilon\right)(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})+C\varepsilon G^{new}+C(\delta_{0}+\varepsilon)(G^{BL}+G^{S}+B^{C})$ $\displaystyle+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R}+\varepsilon)G^{R}.$ Proof. First, we split $B^{rem}(U)$ as $\displaystyle B^{rem}(U)$ $\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}|Y_{3}(U)|^{2}+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{3}\,d\xi+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\quad+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|u_{\xi}^{S}|\,d\xi+C\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\bar{u}_{\xi}|\,d\xi+\mathcal{J}^{P}(U),$ $\displaystyle:=\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}|Y_{3}(U)|^{2}+B^{rem}_{1}+B^{rem}_{2}+B^{rem}_{3}+B^{rem}_{4}+\mathcal{J}^{P}(U).$ $\bullet$ Estimates of $Y_{3}(U)$ : From $|u_{\xi}|\leq C\delta_{S}^{1/2}|a_{\xi}|$ , note that $\displaystyle|Y_{3}(U)|$ $\displaystyle=\left|-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-R(\theta^{S})_{\xi}\Phi\left(\frac{v}{\bar{v}}\right)-\frac{R}{\gamma-1}(\theta^{S})_{\xi}\Phi\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}\right)\right]\,d\xi\right|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi.$ Using ( 3.2 ), Lemma 2.5 , and ( 3.4 ), we obtain $\displaystyle\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}|Y_{3}|^{2}\leq$ $\displaystyle\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|a_{\xi}||(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}d\xi\right)^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}||(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}d\xi\right)^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq$ $\displaystyle\|(\phi,\psi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}||(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}d\xi\leq C\varepsilon^{2}G^{S}.$ Here, we used the fact that $\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{S}||(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi\leq C\delta_{S}^{2}\|(\phi,\psi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.$ $\bullet$ Estimates of $B^{rem}_{1},B^{rem}_{2},B^{rem}_{3}$ : First, by ( 3.3 ) and the interpolation inequality, we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} B^{rem}_{1}=&\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{3}d\xi\\
\leq&C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|a_{\xi}|\Big|\phi+\frac{\psi}{\sigma^{*}}\Big|^{3}d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|a_{\xi}||\psi|^{3}d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|a_{\xi}|\Big|\chi-\frac{(\gamma-1)\theta^{*}}{v^{*}\sigma^{*}}\psi\Big|^{3}d\xi\\
\leq&C\varepsilon(G_{1}+G_{2})+C\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta_{S}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}|\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|\psi|d\xi\\
\leq&C\varepsilon(G_{1}+G_{2})+C\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta_{S}}}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}|\psi^{2}d\xi}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}|d\xi}\\
\leq&C\varepsilon(G_{1}+G_{2})+C\varepsilon\sqrt{D}\sqrt{G^{S}}\leq C\varepsilon(G_{1}+G_{2}+D+G^{S}).\end{aligned}$ Again using $|u_{\xi}|\leq C\delta_{S}|a_{\xi}|$ and ( 3.3 ), we obtain $B^{rem}_{2}+B^{rem}_{3}\leq C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi\leq C\sqrt{\delta_{S}}G^{S},$ Using $\bar{u}_{\xi}=u^{BL}_{\xi}+u^{R}_{\xi}+u^{C}_{\xi}+u^{S}_{\xi}$ and Lemma 2.4 , we have $\displaystyle B^{rem}_{4}\leq C\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}|\bar{u}_{\xi}|\,d\xi\leq C\varepsilon\left(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}\right)+C\varepsilon B^{C}.$ $\bullet$ Estimates of $\mathcal{J}^{P}(U)$ : We first decompose $\mathcal{J}^{P}(U)$ as follows: $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{P}$ $\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a_{\xi}\left[\mu(u-\bar{u})\left(\frac{u_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)+\kappa\frac{\theta-\bar{\theta}}{\theta}\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)\right]\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\left[-\mu\bar{u}_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)(u-\bar{u})_{\xi}+\kappa\frac{\theta-\bar{\theta}}{\theta^{2}}\theta_{\xi}\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)-\kappa\frac{(\theta-\bar{\theta})_{\xi}}{\theta}\bar{\theta}_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)\right.$ $\displaystyle\phantom{+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\Big[\quad}\left.+\mu\frac{\theta-\bar{\theta}}{\theta}\left(\frac{u_{\xi}^{2}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)-\frac{(\theta-\bar{\theta})^{2}}{\theta\bar{\theta}}\left(\kappa\left(\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}+\mu\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)\right]\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle:=\mathcal{J}_{1}^{P}+\mathcal{J}_{2}^{P}.$ Using Young’s inequality and ( 3.3 ), we obtain $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{J}_{1}^{P}&\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|a_{\xi}|\psi|\Big(|\psi_{\xi}|+|\bar{u}_{\xi}||\phi|\Big)d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|a_{\xi}||\chi|\Big(|\chi_{\xi}|+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi}||\phi|\Big)d\xi\\
&\leq\frac{1}{100}D_{u_{1}}+\frac{1}{100}D_{\theta_{1}}+C\frac{1}{\delta_{S}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}|^{2}|(\psi,\chi)|^{2}d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\bar{u}_{\xi}|^{2}+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi}|^{2})|\phi|^{2}d\xi\\
&\leq\frac{1}{100}D_{u_{1}}+\frac{1}{100}D_{\theta_{1}}+C\delta_{S}G^{S}+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\bar{u}_{\xi}|^{2}+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi}|^{2})|\phi|^{2}d\xi.\end{aligned}$ Similarly, applying Young’s inequality, we have the following estimate for $\mathcal{J}_{2}^{P}$ : $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{J}_{2}^{P}\leq&\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big[|\bar{u}_{\xi}||\phi||\psi_{\xi}|+|\chi|\Big(|\chi_{\xi}|+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi}|\Big)\Big(|\chi_{\xi}|+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi}||\phi|\Big)\Big]d\xi\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big[|\chi_{\xi}||\bar{\theta}_{\xi}||\phi|+|\chi|\Big(|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}+|\bar{u}_{\xi}||\psi_{\xi}|+|\bar{u}_{\xi}|^{2}|\phi|\Big)\Big]d\xi\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(|\bar{\theta}_{\xi\xi}|+|\bar{v}_{\xi}\bar{\theta}_{\xi}|+|\bar{u}_{\xi}|^{2}\right)|\chi|^{2}\,d\xi\\
\leq&\frac{1}{100}(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\bar{u}_{\xi}|^{2}+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi}|^{2}+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi\xi}|)|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}d\xi.\end{aligned}$ By Lemma 2.1 , Lemma 2.3 , ( 2.8 ), ( 2.11 ), we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\bar{u}_{\xi}|^{2}+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi}|^{2}+|\bar{\theta}_{\xi\xi}|)|\phi|^{2}d\xi\leq C\left(\delta_{BL}B^{BL}+\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R}G^{R}+\delta_{S}G^{S}\right)+C\delta_{C}B^{C}.\end{aligned}$ Thus, we have $\mathcal{J}^{P}=\mathcal{J}^{P}_{1}+\mathcal{J}^{P}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{50}(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})+C(\delta_{BL}G^{BL}+\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R}G^{R}+\delta_{S}G^{S})+C\delta_{C}B^{C}.$ Combining all these estimates, we obtain the desired result in Lemma 5.9 .
∎ The following lemma provides an estimates for $B^{C}$ . Lemma 5.10 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exists a positive constant $C$ such that the following estimate holds. $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|B^{C}|\,dt$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{C}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(U-\overline{U})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{T}(\delta_{S}|\dot{X}|^{2}+G^{S}+G^{R}+G^{BL})\,dt$ $\displaystyle+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{T}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}}+D_{u_{2}}+D_{\theta_{2}})\,dt+C\delta_{C}.$ Proof. Since the proof of Lemma 5.10 is almost similar to those in [ 14 ] and [ 29 , Appendix D] , we defer it to Appendix A .
∎ 5.4.2. Estimates of $B^{I}(U)$ Lemma 5.11 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exists a positive constant $C$ such that, for any $t\in[0,T]$ , $\int_{0}^{t}|B^{I}|\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{0}^{1/3}+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{1/9}+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}D_{v_{1}}\,d\tau+\frac{1}{40}\int_{0}^{t}(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,d\tau.$ Proof. We decompose $B^{I}(U)$ as follows: $\displaystyle B^{I}(U)$ $\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}|Y_{2}(U)|^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}aQ_{1}(u-\bar{u})\,d\xi-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}aQ_{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{\bar{\theta}}-1\right)\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big(|(v^{R}-v_{*},\theta^{R}-\theta_{*})||u^{BL}_{\xi}|+|a_{\xi}||(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})|\big)|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle:=\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}|Y_{2}(U)|^{2}+B^{I}_{1}+B^{I}_{2}+B^{I}_{3}+B^{I}_{4}.$ $\bullet$ Estimates of $Y_{2}(U)$ : Observe that $\displaystyle|Y_{2}(U)|$ $\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a(v^{S})_{\xi}\left(\frac{\bar{p}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{p^{*}}{v^{*}}\right)\phi\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\frac{R}{\gamma-1}(\theta^{S})_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{\bar{\theta}}-\frac{1}{\theta^{*}}\right)\chi\,d\xi\right|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(v^{S}_{\xi},\theta^{S}_{\xi})||(\bar{v}-v^{*},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{*})||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(v^{S}_{\xi},\theta^{S}_{\xi})||(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(v^{S}_{\xi},\theta^{S}_{\xi})||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi.$ Using ( 1.21 ), $|v_{\xi}^{S}|\sim|u_{\xi}^{S}|\sim|\theta_{\xi}^{S}|$ and the fact that $\displaystyle|v^{BL}-v_{*}|\sim|\theta^{BL}-\theta_{*}|,\quad|v^{R}-v_{m}|\sim|\theta^{R}-\theta_{m}|,\quad|v^{C}-v^{*}|\sim|\theta^{C}-\theta^{*}|,$ we have $\displaystyle|Y_{2}(U)|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{S}|\big(|v^{BL}-v_{*}|+|v^{R}-v_{m}|+|v^{C}-v^{*}|\big)\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{S}||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(J_{3}+J_{5}+J_{6}\big)\,d\xi+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi.$ where $J_{3},J_{5}$ , and $J_{6}$ are defined in ( 4.1 ). Using the interpolation inequality, ( 3.4 ), and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain $\displaystyle\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}Y_{2}^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}\|(\phi_{\xi},\chi_{\xi})\|_{L^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(J_{3}+J_{5}+J_{6})\,d\xi\right)^{2}+C\delta_{S}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}||(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi\right)^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}}\varepsilon\|(\phi_{\xi},\chi_{\xi})\|_{L^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(J_{3}+J_{5}+J_{6})\,d\xi\right)^{2}+C\delta_{S}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}|\,d\xi\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{S}_{\xi}||(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})+\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(J_{3}+J_{5}+J_{6})\,d\xi\right)^{4}+C\delta_{S}^{2}G^{S}.$ By Lemma 4.1 , we find that $\displaystyle\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(J_{3}+J_{5}+J_{6})\,d\xi\right)^{4}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2}}\left[(\delta_{BL}^{4}\delta_{S}^{3}+\delta_{BL}^{3}\delta_{S}^{4})+\left(\delta_{R}^{4}\delta_{S}^{3}(\varepsilon_{r}+\delta_{S})^{4}+\frac{(\delta_{R}\delta_{S})^{4}}{\varepsilon_{r}^{5}}(\varepsilon_{r}+\delta_{S})^{4}\right)+(\delta_{C}^{4}\delta_{S}^{3}+\delta_{C}^{4}\delta_{S}^{4})\right]$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{S}+C\delta_{S}^{2}\frac{\delta_{R}^{4}}{\varepsilon_{r}^{5}}.$ $\bullet$ Estimates of $B^{I}_{1},B^{I}_{2}$ : From ( 5.2 ), $\displaystyle B_{1}^{I}+B_{2}^{I}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}aQ_{1}(u-\bar{u})\,d\xi+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}aQ_{2}\left(1-\frac{\bar{\theta}}{\theta}\right)\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|Q_{i}^{I}||(\psi,\chi)|\,d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|Q_{i}^{R}||(\psi,\chi)|\,d\xi+C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|Q_{i}^{C}||(\psi,\chi)|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle:=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.$ For $I_{1}$ , we use the interpolation inequality, Young’s inequality, and ( 3.4 ) to have $\displaystyle|I_{1}|$ $\displaystyle\leq\|(\psi,\chi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|Q_{i}^{I}|\,d\xi\leq\|(\psi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|(\psi_{\xi},\chi_{\xi})\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|Q_{i}^{I}|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})+C\varepsilon^{2/3}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|Q_{i}^{I}\|_{L^{1}}^{4/3}.$ Recall that the term $Q_{1}^{I}$ in ( 5.3 ) defined by $Q_{1}^{I}=\big(\bar{p}-p^{BL}-p^{R}-p^{C}-p^{S}\big)_{\xi}-\mu\left(\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{u^{BL}_{\xi}}{v^{BL}}-\frac{u^{R}_{\xi}}{v^{R}}-\frac{u^{C}_{\xi}}{v^{C}}-\frac{u^{S}_{\xi}}{v^{S}}\right)_{\xi}.$ For the first term of $Q_{1}^{I}$ , using $|(v_{i})_{\xi}|\sim|(\theta_{i})_{\xi}|$ and $|\bar{v}-v_{i}|\sim|\bar{\theta}-\theta_{i}|$ for $i=1,2,3,4$ , we find that $\big(\bar{p}-p^{BL}-p^{R}-p^{C}-p^{S}\big)_{\xi}\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(v_{i})_{\xi}||\bar{v}-v_{i}|.$ Moreover, we obtain $\displaystyle\left(\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{u^{BL}_{\xi}}{v^{BL}}-\frac{u^{R}_{\xi}}{v^{R}}-\frac{u^{C}_{\xi}}{v^{C}}-\frac{u^{S}_{\xi}}{v^{S}}\right)_{\xi}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}\big(|(u_{i})_{\xi\xi}|,|(u_{i})_{\xi}||(v_{i})_{\xi}|\big)|\bar{v}-v_{i}|+\sum_{i\neq j}|(u_{i})_{\xi}||(v_{j})_{\xi}|.$ Therefore, we have (5.42) $|Q_{1}^{I}|\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(v_{i})_{\xi}||\bar{v}-v_{i}|+|u_{\xi\xi}^{C}||\bar{v}-v^{C}|+\sum_{i\neq j}|(u_{i})_{\xi}||(v_{j})_{\xi}|.$ In the above estimate, we have used the estimates $|(u_{i})_{\xi\xi}|\leq C|(u_{i})_{\xi}|$ and $|(u_{i})_{\xi}|\sim|(v_{i})_{\xi}|$ for $i=1,2,4$ , which follow directly from Lemmas 2.1 , 2.3 , and 2.5 . In addition, we recall that $\displaystyle Q_{2}^{I}$ $\displaystyle:=\Big(\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi}-p^{BL}u^{BL}_{\xi}-p^{R}u_{\xi}^{R}-p^{C}u_{\xi}^{C}-p^{S}_{\xi}u^{S}_{\xi})-\kappa\left(\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{\theta^{BL}_{\xi}}{v^{BL}}-\frac{\theta^{R}_{\xi}}{v^{R}}-\frac{\theta^{C}_{\xi}}{v^{C}}-\frac{\theta^{S}_{\xi}}{v^{S}}\right)_{\xi}$ $\displaystyle\quad-\mu\left(\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}-\frac{(u^{BL}_{\xi})^{2}}{v^{BL}}-\frac{(u^{R}_{\xi})^{2}}{v^{R}}-\frac{(u^{C}_{\xi})^{2}}{v^{C}}-\frac{(u^{S}_{\xi})^{2}}{v^{S}}\right)=:L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3}.$ Note that $\displaystyle|L_{1}|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(u_{i})_{\xi}||\bar{p}-p_{i}|\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(v_{i})_{\xi}||\bar{v}-v_{i}|+|u_{\xi}^{C}||\bar{v}-v^{C}|.$ Using calculations similar to those for $Q_{1}^{I}$ , we obtain $|L_{2}|\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(v_{i})_{\xi}||\bar{v}-v_{i}|+C(|v_{\xi}^{C}|^{2}+|u_{\xi}^{C}|)|\bar{v}-v^{C}|+\sum_{i\neq j}|(v_{i})_{\xi}||(v_{j})_{\xi}|,$ where we have used $|(v_{i})_{\xi}|\sim|(\theta_{i})_{\xi}|$ , $|(v_{i})_{\xi\xi}|\sim|(\theta_{i})_{\xi\xi}|$ for $i=1,2,3,4$ , together with $|(v_{i})_{\xi\xi}|\leq C|(v_{i})_{\xi}|$ for $i=1,2,4$ , and for the viscous contact wave, $|\theta^{C}_{\xi\xi}|\leq C|\theta^{C}_{\xi}|^{2}+C|u^{C}_{\xi}|,$ which follows from ( 1.16 ) 2 . Finally, we have $\displaystyle|L_{3}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(u_{i})_{\xi}|^{2}|\bar{v}-v_{i}|+C\sum_{i\neq j}|(u_{i})_{\xi}||(u_{j})_{\xi}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(v_{i})_{\xi}||\bar{v}-v_{i}|+C|u_{\xi}^{C}|^{2}|\bar{v}-v^{C}|+C\sum_{i\neq j}|(u_{i})_{\xi}||(u_{j})_{\xi}|.$ Therefore, we obtain (5.43) $|Q_{2}^{I}|\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(v_{i})_{\xi}||\bar{v}-v_{i}|+C|u_{\xi}^{C}||\bar{v}-v^{C}|+C\sum_{i\neq j}|(u_{i})_{\xi}|\big(|(v_{j})_{\xi}|+|(u_{j})_{\xi}|\big).$ Using the fact that $\sum_{i=1}^{4}|(v_{i})_{\xi}||\bar{v}-v_{i}|\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{6}J_{i},$ where $J_{i}$ are defined in ( 4.1 ),
and combining ( 5.42 ) and ( 5.43 ), we obtain $|Q_{1}^{I}|+|Q_{2}^{I}|\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{6}J_{i}+C|(u_{\xi}^{C},u_{\xi\xi}^{C})||\bar{v}-v^{C}|+C\sum_{i\neq j}|(u_{i})_{\xi}|\big(|(v_{j})_{\xi}|+|(u_{j})_{\xi}|\big).$ Therefore, using ( 4.3 ) and ( 4.14 ), we obtain $\int_{0}^{t}\||Q_{1}^{I}|+|Q_{2}^{I}|\|_{L^{1}}^{4/3}\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{0}+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{1/6}+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}.$ Thus, we have $\int_{0}^{t}|I_{1}|\,d\tau\leq\frac{1}{100}(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})+C\delta_{0}+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{1/6}+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}.$ For $I_{2}$ , following the calculations in $I_{1}$ , we find that $|I_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{100}(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})+C\varepsilon^{2/3}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|Q_{i}^{R}\|_{L^{1}}^{4/3}.$ It follows from ( 5.4 ) that (5.44) $\displaystyle|Q^{R}_{1}|+|Q_{2}^{R}|\leq C$ $\displaystyle\left[|u^{R}_{\xi\xi}|+|u^{R}_{\xi}||v^{R}_{\xi}|+|\theta^{R}_{\xi\xi}|+|\theta^{R}_{\xi}||v^{R}_{\xi}|+|u^{R}_{\xi}|^{2}\right]$ $\displaystyle\leq C$ $\displaystyle\left[\big|\big(u^{R}_{\xi\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi\xi}\big)\big|+\big|\big(v^{R}_{\xi},u^{R}_{\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi}\big)\big|^{2}\right],$ which implies $\||Q^{R}_{1}|+|Q^{R}_{2}|\|_{L^{1}}\leq C\Big[\big\|\big(u^{R}_{\xi\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi\xi}\big)\big\|_{L^{1}}+\big\|\big(v^{R}_{\xi},u^{R}_{\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi}\big)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big].$ By virtue of Lemma 2.3 , we have $\displaystyle\big\|\big(u^{R}_{\xi\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi\xi}\big)\big\|_{L^{1}}\leq\big\|\big(u^{R}_{\xi\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi\xi}\big)\big\|^{\frac{1}{9}}_{L^{1}}\big\|\big(u^{R}_{\xi\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi\xi}\big)\big\|^{\frac{8}{9}}_{L^{1}}\leq C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{\frac{1}{9}}(\delta_{R}^{\frac{8}{9}}+\delta_{R}^{\frac{1}{9}})(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{9}},$ where we have taken $q=8$ in Lemma 2.3 , and $\displaystyle\big\|\big(v^{R}_{\xi},u^{R}_{\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi}\big)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\big\|\big(v^{R}_{\xi},u^{R}_{\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi}\big)\big\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}}\big\|\big(v^{R}_{\xi},u^{R}_{\xi},\theta^{R}_{\xi}\big)\big\|^{\frac{7}{4}}_{L^{2}}\leq C(\varepsilon_{r}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{R})^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta_{R}^{\frac{7}{8}}(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{8}}.$ Combining the above estimates, we obtain $\displaystyle\||Q^{R}_{1}|+|Q^{R}_{2}|\|^{\frac{4}{3}}_{L^{1}}\leq$ $\displaystyle C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{\frac{4}{27}}\delta_{R}^{\frac{32}{27}}(1+t)^{-\frac{28}{27}}+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{\frac{1}{6}}\delta_{R}^{\frac{4}{3}}(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{6}},$ and therefore, $\int_{0}^{t}\||Q^{R}_{1}|+|Q^{R}_{2}|\|^{\frac{4}{3}}_{L^{1}}\,d\tau\leq C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{1/9}.$ Finally, for $I_{3}$ , we use Hölder’s inequality and ( 3.4 ) to have $\displaystyle|I_{3}|\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}|Q_{i}^{C}||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|Q_{i}^{C}\|_{L^{2}}\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|Q_{i}^{C}\|_{L^{2}}.$ Since it follows from ( 2.9 ) that $\displaystyle\|Q_{1}^{C}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\delta_{C}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}},\qquad\|Q_{2}^{C}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\delta_{C}(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{4}},$ we obtain $\int_{0}^{t}|I_{3}|\,d\xi\leq C\delta_{C}.$ Combining the above estimates, we have $\int_{0}^{t}|B_{1}^{I}+B_{2}^{I}|\,d\tau\leq\frac{1}{50}(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})+C\delta_{0}+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{1/9}+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}.$ $\bullet$ Estimates of $B^{I}_{3}$ : By the interpolation inequality, we obtain $\displaystyle|B_{3}^{I}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\||u_{\xi}^{BL}||(v^{R}-v_{*},\theta^{R}-\theta_{*})|\|_{L^{2}}\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{4}}^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\||u_{\xi}^{BL}||(v^{R}-v_{*},\theta^{R}-\theta_{*})|\|_{L^{2}}\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}^{3/2}\|(\phi_{\xi},\chi_{\xi})\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|J_{1}|\|_{L^{2}}^{4/3}+C\varepsilon^{6}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}}),$ where $J_{1}$ is defined in ( 4.1 ). Using Lemma 4.3 , we have $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|B_{3}^{I}|\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{R}^{1/6}(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{1/12}\delta_{BL}^{5/6}+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{1/4}\delta_{BL}+C\varepsilon^{6}\int_{0}^{t}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{0}^{5/6}+C\varepsilon^{6}\int_{0}^{t}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,d\tau.$ $\bullet$ Estimates of $B^{I}_{4}$ : By ( 3.2 ) and the interpolation inequality, we have $\displaystyle|B_{4}^{I}|$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{1/2}}\||u^{S}_{\xi}||(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})|\|_{L^{2}}\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{4}}^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{1/2}}\||u^{S}_{\xi}||(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})|\|_{L^{2}}\|\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}^{3/2}\|(\phi_{\xi},\chi_{\xi})\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2/3}}\||u^{S}_{\xi}||(\bar{v}-v^{S},\bar{\theta}-\theta^{S})|\|_{L^{2}}^{4/3}+\varepsilon^{6}\|(\phi_{\xi},\chi_{\xi})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.$ Using the same arguments as for $Y_{2}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle|B_{4}^{I}|$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2/3}}\||u^{S}_{\xi}|(|v^{BL}-v_{*}|+|v^{R}-v_{m}|+|v^{C}-v^{*}|)\|_{L^{2}}^{4/3}+C\varepsilon^{6}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}}).$ Using ( 4.20 ), ( 4.21 ) and ( 4.22 ), we obtain $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|B_{4}^{I}|d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2/3}}(\delta_{BL}^{4/3}\delta_{S}+\delta_{BL}^{1/3}\delta_{S}^{2})+\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2/3}}\left(\delta_{R}^{4/3}\delta_{S}+\delta_{S}^{8/3}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)$ $\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\delta_{S}^{2/3}}(\delta_{S}\delta_{C}^{8/3}+\delta_{C}^{4/3}\delta_{S}^{8/3})$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{0}^{1/3}+C\delta_{0}\delta_{R}^{4/3}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}+C\varepsilon^{6}\int_{0}^{t}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,d\tau.$ Combining the above estimates and using $\varepsilon_{r}<1$ , we conclude that $\int_{0}^{t}|B^{I}|\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{0}^{1/3}+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{1/9}+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}D_{v_{1}}\,d\tau+\frac{1}{40}\int_{0}^{t}(D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,d\tau.$ ∎ 5.4.3. Estimates of $\mathcal{J}^{bd}$ Lemma 5.12 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exist positive constants $c$ and $C$ such that $\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}^{bd}\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{C}+Ce^{-c\delta_{S}\beta}+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\chi_{\xi\xi}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\,d\tau.$ Proof. First, we decompose $\mathcal{J}^{bd}$ into three terms as follows: $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{1}:=-\sigma_{-}a\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\Big|_{\xi=0}+a(u-\bar{u})(p-\bar{p})\Big|_{\xi=0},$ $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{2}:=-a\mu\left[(u-\bar{u})\left(\frac{u_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)\right]\Big|_{\xi=0},$ $\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{3}:=-a\kappa\left[\frac{\theta-\bar{\theta}}{\theta}\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)\right]\Big|_{\xi=0}.$ By ( 5.5 ), we have (5.45) $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}_{1}^{bd}d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|(\phi,\psi,\chi)|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}d\tau.$ By definition of $\overline{U}$ in ( 1.21 ), we have $\displaystyle|U-\overline{U}|\big|_{\xi=0}=|U^{C}+U^{S}-U_{m}-U^{*}|\big|_{\xi=0}\leq|U^{C}-U_{m}|\big|_{\xi=0}+|U^{S}-U^{*}|\big|_{\xi=0}.$ From Lemma 2.4 , it follows that (5.46) $\displaystyle|U^{C}-U_{m}|\big|_{\xi=0}\leq C\delta_{C}e^{\frac{-C_{1}(\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}}\leq C\delta_{C}e^{-Ct}.$ On the other hand, by $|X(t)|\leq C\varepsilon t$ and taking $\varepsilon>0$ sufficiently small, we obtain $-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta\leq-\frac{(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t}{2}-\beta<0.$ Therefore, by Lemma 2.5 , we have (5.47) $|U^{S}-U^{*}|\big|_{\xi=0}\leq|U^{S}(-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)-U^{*}|\leq C\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta},$ Substituting ( 5.46 ) and ( 5.47 ) into ( 5.45 ), we obtain (5.48) $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{1}d\tau\leq C\delta_{C}^{2}+C\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}.$ For $\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{2}$ , observe that (5.49) $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{2}d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|\psi\psi_{\xi}|\big|_{\xi=0}d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}|\psi\phi\bar{u}_{\xi}|\big|_{\xi=0}d\tau.\end{aligned}$ For the first term on the right-hand side of ( 5.49 ), we use the interpolation inequality, Young’s inequality, and ( 3.4 ) to obtain $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\psi\psi_{\xi}|\big|_{\xi=0}d\tau\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}}|\psi|\big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}|\psi|\big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|\psi(\tau,0)|^{4/3}d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\delta_{C}+\delta_{S}^{1/3}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta})+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}d\tau.$ For the last inequality, we used ( 5.46 ) and ( 5.47 ), which yield $\int_{0}^{t}|\psi(\tau,0)|^{4/3}d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|u^{C}-u_{m}|^{4/3}+|u^{S}-u^{*}|^{4/3}\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{C}^{4/3}+C\delta_{S}^{1/3}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta},$ For the second term on the right-hand side of ( 5.49 ), we use ( 5.45 ) and ( 5.48 ) to have $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\psi\phi\bar{u}_{\xi}|\big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|(\phi,\psi)|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{C}^{2}+Ce^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}.$ Therefore, we have $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{2}d\tau\leq C(\delta_{C}+\delta_{S}^{1/3}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta})+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|(u-\bar{u})_{\xi\xi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}d\tau.$ Similarly, $\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{3}$ can be estimated as $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}^{bd}_{3}d\tau\leq C(\delta_{C}+\delta_{S}^{1/3}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta})+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|(\theta-\bar{\theta})_{\xi\xi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}d\tau.$ ∎ 5.4.4. Conclusion By Lemma 5.5 and Lemma 5.6 , we have $\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\delta_{S}}{2M}|\dot{X}(t)|^{2}+CB^{C}(U)+B^{S}(U)+B^{rem}(U)+B^{I}(U)+\mathcal{J}^{bd}(U)$ $\displaystyle\quad-C_{2}(G^{BL}(U)+G^{R}(U))-G^{new}(U)-D(U)$ $\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}(t)|^{2}+B^{rem}(U)+B^{I}(U)$ $\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{J}^{bd}(U)-C_{2}(G^{BL}(U)+G^{R}(U))-\frac{1}{4}G^{new}(U)-\frac{1}{4}D(U).$ Using Lemma 5.9 and the smallness of $\delta_{0},\varepsilon_{0}$ , and $\varepsilon_{r}$ , we have $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}a\bar{\theta}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi\leq&-\frac{\delta_{S}}{4M}|\dot{X}(t)|^{2}-\frac{1}{10}G^{new}(U)-\frac{C_{S}}{2}G^{S}(U)-\frac{C_{2}}{2}(G^{BL}(U)+G^{R}(U))\\
&\quad-\frac{1}{10}D(U)+B^{I}(U)+\mathcal{J}^{bd}(U)+CB^{C}(U).\end{aligned}$ Integrating the above inequality over $[0,T]$ , and using Lemma 5.11 and Lemma 5.12 we obtain $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi+\delta_{S}\int_{0}^{T}|\dot{X}|^{2}\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,dt\\
&\quad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\eta(U_{0}|\overline{U}(0,\cdot))\,d\xi+C\delta_{0}^{1/3}+C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})^{1/9}+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}+Ce^{-c\delta_{S}\beta}\\
&\quad\quad+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{u_{2}}+D_{\theta_{2}})\,dt+C\int_{0}^{T}B^{C}\,dt.\end{aligned}$ Now, choosing $\varepsilon_{r}>0$ as (5.50) $\varepsilon_{r}:=\delta_{0}^{1/4},$ and using Lemma 5.10 and the fact that $\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\eta(U|\overline{U})\,d\xi\sim\|U-\overline{U}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},$ we obtain the desired estimate in Lemma 5.1 . 6. Higher Order Estimates In this section, we provide the higher order estimates and complete the proof of Proposition 3.2 . It follows from ( 1.3 ) and ( 5.1 ) that the equations for the perturbations $(\phi,\psi,\chi)$ are given by (6.1) $\begin{cases}&\phi_{t}-\sigma_{-}\phi_{\xi}-\psi_{\xi}=\dot{X}(t)v^{S}_{\xi},\quad t>0,\quad\xi>0,\\
&\psi_{t}-\sigma_{-}\psi_{\xi}+(p-\bar{p})_{\xi}=\mu\left(\frac{u_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}-Q_{1}+\dot{X}(t)u_{\xi}^{S},\\
&\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\big(\chi_{t}-\sigma_{-}\chi_{\xi}\big)+(pu_{\xi}-\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi})=\kappa\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}+\mu\left(\frac{u_{\xi}^{2}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)-Q_{2}+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\dot{X}(t)\theta^{S}_{\xi}.\end{cases}$ 6.1. Higher Order Estimates on $v-\bar{v}$ Lemma 6.1 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , and for any $\nu>0$ , there exist positive constants $c$ , $C>0$ (independent of $\nu$ ), and $C_{\nu}>0$ such that $\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\phi_{\xi}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}D_{v_{1}}\,dt$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C\big(\|\psi(0,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\phi_{\xi}(0,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)+C\int_{0}^{T}(\delta_{S}|\dot{X}|^{2}+G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,dt$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C_{\nu}\int_{0}^{T}D_{u_{1}}\,dt+C(\nu+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{T}D_{u_{2}}\,dt+C\int_{0}^{T}B^{C}\,dt+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|Q_{1}|^{2}\,d\xi\,dt$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}^{1/2}+\delta_{C}^{2}+e^{-c\delta_{S}\beta}).$ Proof. Before proving Lemma 6.1 , we denote $D_{v}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{R\theta\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{v}\,d\xi\sim D_{v_{1}}.$ Differentiating ( 6.1 ) 1 with respect to $\xi$ and multiplying $\mu\phi_{\xi}$ , we have (6.2) $\mu\left(\frac{\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{2}\right)_{t}-\sigma_{-}\mu\left(\frac{\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{2}\right)_{\xi}=\mu\dot{X}v^{S}_{\xi\xi}\phi_{\xi}+\mu\phi_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}.$ In order to remove the term $\mu\phi_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}$ , we make use of the momentum equation as follows. Multiplying ( 6.1 ) 2 by $-v\phi_{\xi}$ , we obtain (6.3) $\displaystyle-v\phi_{\xi}\psi_{t}+\sigma_{-}v\phi_{\xi}\psi_{\xi}$ $\displaystyle=-\dot{X}v\phi_{\xi}u^{S}_{\xi}+v\phi_{\xi}(p-\bar{p})_{\xi}-\mu\phi_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}+\mu\frac{\phi_{\xi}\psi_{\xi}v_{\xi}}{v}$ $\displaystyle+\mu\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi\xi}\phi_{\xi}\phi}{\bar{v}}+\mu v\phi_{\xi}\bar{u}_{\xi}\left(\frac{v_{\xi}}{v^{2}}-\frac{\bar{v}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}^{2}}\right)+v\phi_{\xi}Q_{1}.$ Here, we used the fact that $\left(\frac{u_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}=\frac{\psi_{\xi\xi}}{v}-\frac{\psi_{\xi}v_{\xi}}{v^{2}}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi\xi}\phi}{v\bar{v}}-\bar{u}_{\xi}\left(\frac{v_{\xi}}{v^{2}}-\frac{\bar{v}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}^{2}}\right).$ By adding ( 6.2 ) and ( 6.3 ), we obtain (6.4) $\displaystyle\left(\mu\frac{\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{2}-v\psi\phi_{\xi}\right)_{t}-\left(\mu\sigma_{-}\frac{\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{2}-v\psi\phi_{t}\right)_{\xi}+(v_{t}-\sigma_{-}v_{\xi})\psi\phi_{\xi}-(v_{\xi}\psi+v\psi_{\xi})(\phi_{t}-\sigma_{-}\phi_{\xi})$ $\displaystyle=\dot{X}\phi_{\xi}\big(\mu v^{S}_{\xi\xi}-vu_{\xi}^{S})+v\phi_{\xi}(p-\bar{p})_{\xi}+\mu\frac{\phi_{\xi}\psi_{\xi}v_{\xi}}{v}+\mu\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi\xi}\phi_{\xi}\phi}{\bar{v}}+\mu v\phi_{\xi}\bar{u}_{\xi}\left(\frac{v_{\xi}}{v^{2}}-\frac{\bar{v}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}^{2}}\right)+v\phi_{\xi}Q_{1}.$ From ( 1.3 ) and ( 6.1 ), we find that $(v_{t}-\sigma_{-}v_{\xi})\psi\phi_{\xi}-(v_{\xi}\psi+v\psi_{\xi})(\phi_{t}-\sigma_{-}\phi_{\xi})=-\dot{X}v_{\xi}^{S}(v_{\xi}\psi+v\psi_{\xi})+(u_{\xi}\psi\phi_{\xi}-v_{\xi}\psi\psi_{\xi}-v\psi_{\xi}^{2}).$ Combining this with the identity: $(p-\bar{p})_{\xi}=-\frac{R\theta\phi_{\xi}}{v^{2}}+\frac{R\chi_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{R\bar{\theta}_{\xi}\phi}{v\bar{v}}-R\bar{v}_{\xi}\left(\frac{\theta}{v^{2}}-\frac{\bar{\theta}}{\bar{v}^{2}}\right),$ and integrating ( 6.4 ) over $[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\mu\frac{|\phi_{\xi}|^{2}}{2}-v\psi\phi_{\xi}\right)\,d\xi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{R\theta\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{v}\,d\xi\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\left.\left(\mu\sigma_{-}\frac{\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{2}-v\psi\phi_{t}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad=:\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\mu\frac{|\phi_{\xi}|^{2}}{2}-v\psi\phi_{t}\right)\bigg|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau+\sum_{i=1}^{5}N_{i},$ where $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &N_{1}=\int_{0}^{t}\dot{X}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big(v_{\xi}^{S}(v_{\xi}\psi+v\psi_{\xi})+\phi_{\xi}(\mu v^{S}_{\xi\xi}-vu_{\xi}^{S})\Big)\,d\xi\,d\tau,\\
&N_{2}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(-u_{\xi}\psi\phi_{\xi}+v_{\xi}\psi\psi_{\xi}+v\psi_{\xi}^{2})\,d\xi\,d\tau,\end{aligned}$ $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &N_{3}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left[R\phi_{\xi}\chi_{\xi}-\frac{R\bar{\theta}_{\xi}\phi\phi_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}-R\phi_{\xi}\bar{v}_{\xi}\left(\frac{\theta}{v^{2}}-\frac{\bar{\theta}}{\bar{v}^{2}}\right)+\mu\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi\xi}\phi_{\xi}\phi}{\bar{v}}\right.\\
&\phantom{N_{3}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big[}\left.+\mu v\phi_{\xi}\bar{u}_{\xi}\left(\frac{v_{\xi}}{v^{2}}-\frac{\bar{v}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}^{2}}\right)\right]d\xi d\tau,\end{aligned}$ $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &N_{4}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mu\frac{\phi_{\xi}\psi_{\xi}v_{\xi}}{v}\,d\xi\,d\tau,&&N_{5}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}v\phi_{\xi}Q_{1}\,d\xi\,d\tau.\end{aligned}$ $\bullet$ (Estimates of boundary terms)
We first estimate the boundary terms. In order to control the term $\phi_{\xi}^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}$ we use the mass equation ( 6.1 ) 1 to have $|\phi_{\xi}|\big|_{\xi=0}\leq C|\phi_{t}|\big|_{\xi=0}+C|\psi_{\xi}|\big|_{\xi=0}+C|\dot{X}(t)||v^{S}_{\xi}|\big|_{\xi=0}.$ Using $|\dot{X}|\leq C\varepsilon$ and Young’s inequality, we obtain $\left.\left(\mu\sigma_{-}\frac{\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{2}-v\psi\phi_{t}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\leq C|\psi|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}+C|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}+C|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}+C\varepsilon_{1}^{2}|v^{S}_{\xi}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}.$ We can easily find that (6.5) $\int_{0}^{t}|\psi|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C(\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}),\quad\text{and}\quad C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}|v_{\xi}^{S}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\delta_{S}^{3}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}.$ Moreover, thanks to the interpolation inequality, we have $\int_{0}^{t}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\,d\tau\leq C_{\nu}\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau+\nu\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{2}}\,d\tau,$ where $\nu>0$ is an arbitrary constant and $C_{\nu}$ denotes a constant depending on $\nu>0$ . In order to estimate $|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}$ , we use the inflow condition as follows: $|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}=|(\bar{v}(t,0)-v_{-})_{t}|\leq|(v^{C}-v_{m})_{t}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}+|(v^{S}-v^{*})_{t}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}.$ Since we have $|v^{C}_{t}|\big|_{\xi=0}\leq\left|\sigma_{-}v^{C}_{\xi}+u^{C}_{\xi}\right|\leq\left.\frac{C\delta_{C}}{\sqrt{1+t}}e^{-\frac{2C_{1}(\xi+\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}}\right|_{\xi=0}\leq C\delta_{C}e^{-Ct},$ and $|(v^{S}-v^{*})_{t}|\Big|_{\xi=0}\leq C|(v^{S})^{\prime}(-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)||\dot{X}+(\sigma-\sigma_{-})|\leq C\delta_{S}^{2}e^{-C\delta_{S}((\sigma-\sigma_{-})t+\beta)},$ we obtain $\int_{0}^{T}|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big|_{\xi=0}\,dt\leq C(\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}).$ Hence, we have $\int_{0}^{t}\left.\left(\mu\sigma_{-}\frac{\phi_{\xi}^{2}}{2}-v\psi\phi_{t}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C(\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta\beta})+C_{\nu}\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau+\nu\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{2}}\,d\tau.$ $\bullet$ (Estimates of $N_{1}$ )
Using $|\bar{v}_{\xi}|\leq C(\delta_{0}+\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})\leq C$ and $|\psi|\leq C\varepsilon$ , we have $\displaystyle v_{\xi}^{S}(v_{\xi}\psi+v\psi_{\xi})+\phi_{\xi}(\mu v^{S}_{\xi\xi}-vu_{\xi}^{S})$ $\displaystyle\leq C|v_{\xi}^{S}|\big(|\phi_{\xi}|(1+|\psi|)+|\bar{v}_{\xi}||\psi|+|\psi_{\xi}|\big)$ $\displaystyle\leq C|v_{\xi}^{S}|\big(|\phi_{\xi}|+|\psi|+|\psi_{\xi}|).$ Using Young’s inequality, we obtain $|N_{1}|\leq C\delta_{S}\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{X}|^{2}\,d\tau+C\delta_{S}\int_{0}^{t}\big(G^{S}+D_{v}+D_{u_{1}})\,d\tau.$ $\bullet$ (Estimates of $N_{2}$ )
For $N_{2}$ , we use Young’s inequality to have $\displaystyle|N_{2}|$ $\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(v_{\xi}\psi\psi_{\xi}+v\psi_{\xi}^{2}-u_{\xi}\psi\phi_{\xi})\,d\xi\,d\tau\right|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\phi_{\xi}||\psi||\psi_{\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\bar{v}_{\xi},\bar{u}_{\xi})||\psi||\psi_{\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}D_{v}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(\bar{v}_{\xi},\bar{u}_{\xi})|^{2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}D_{v}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(D_{u_{1}}+G^{S})\,d\tau+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|v^{BL}_{\xi}|^{2}+|v^{R}_{\xi}|^{2})|\psi|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau.$ Now, it remains to control the last term of the above inequality. First, we use the interpolation inequality to have $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v^{R}_{\xi}|^{2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|v^{R}_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\,d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\|v^{R}_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\|v^{R}_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\,d\tau+C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau.$ By Lemma 2.3 , we have $\|v^{R}_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\leq\|v^{R}_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\|v^{R}_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{3}\leq C\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}^{1/2}(\delta_{R})^{3/2}(1+t)^{-3/2}.$ Consequently, we obtain $\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v^{R}_{\xi}|^{2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{R}^{5/2}\varepsilon_{r}^{1/2}+C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{2}}\,d\tau.$ On the other hand, in order to control the term related to the boundary layer solution, as in [ 30 ] , we use the Poincaré-type inequality as follows.
