First, note that (6.6) $\psi(\tau,\xi)=\psi(0,\xi)+\int_{0}^{\xi}\psi_{\xi}(\tau,\zeta)\,d\zeta\leq\psi(0,\xi)+\sqrt{\xi}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}.$ Using ( 6.6 ), ( 6.5 ), and Lemma 2.1 , we obtain $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v^{BL}_{\xi}|^{2}|\psi|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|\psi(0,\xi)|^{2}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\xi|v^{BL}_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta})+C\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{\delta_{BL}^{4}\xi}{(1+\delta_{BL}\xi)^{4}}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta})+C\delta_{BL}^{3}\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau.$ Thus, we have $\displaystyle|N_{2}|$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}D_{v}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(D_{u_{1}}+G^{S})\,d\tau+C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle+C(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}^{1/2}+\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}).$ $\bullet$ (Estimates of $N_{3}$ )
Observe that $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |N_{3}|\leq&C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\phi_{\xi}||\chi_{\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\phi_{\xi}||(\bar{\theta}_{\xi},\bar{v}_{\xi},\bar{u}_{\xi\xi},\bar{u}_{\xi}\bar{v}_{\xi})||(\phi,\chi)|\,d\xi\,d\tau\\
&+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{u}_{\xi}||\phi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau.\end{aligned}$ Using Young’s inequality and $|u_{\xi\xi}^{BL}|\leq C|u_{\xi}^{BL}|$ , $|u_{\xi\xi}^{R}|\leq C|u_{\xi}^{R}|$ , $|u_{\xi\xi}^{S}|\leq C|u_{\xi}^{S}|$ , and Lemma 2.4 , we obtain $\displaystyle|N_{3}|\leq\big(\frac{1}{100}+C\delta_{0}+C\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}\big)\int_{0}^{t}D_{v}d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+D_{\theta_{1}})d\tau+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}d\tau.$ $\bullet$ (Estimates of $N_{4}$ )
We use the interpolation inequality to obtain $\displaystyle|N_{4}|$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\phi_{\xi}|^{2}|\psi_{\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\phi_{\xi}||\psi_{\xi}||\bar{v}_{\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\phi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\phi_{\xi}||\psi_{\xi}||\bar{v}_{\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{4/3}\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}D_{v}\,d\tau.$ $\bullet$ (Estimates of $N_{5}$ )
Finally, we have $\displaystyle|N_{5}|\leq$ $\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|Q_{1}|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}D_{v}\,d\tau.$ Therefore, combining all these estimates, together with the fact: $\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(v\psi\phi_{\xi}-v_{0}\psi_{0}\phi_{0\xi}\right)\,d\xi\leq\frac{\mu}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\phi_{\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi+C\big(\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|(\phi_{0\xi},\psi_{0})\|_{L^{2}}\big),$ we obtain the desired estimate in Lemma 6.1 .
∎ 6.2. Higher Order Estimates on $u-\bar{u}$ Lemma 6.2 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exist positive constants $c$ and $C$ such that $\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\psi_{\xi}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}D_{u_{2}}\,dt$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|\psi_{\xi}(0,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{T}\big(\delta_{S}|\dot{X}|^{2}+G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+D_{v_{1}}+D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}}\big)\,dt$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{0}^{T}B^{C}\,dt+C\int_{0}^{T}|Q_{1}|^{2}\,dt+Ce^{-c\delta_{S}\beta}.$ Proof. Throughout the proof of Lemma 6.2 , denote $\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}:=\mu\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{1}{v}|\psi_{\xi\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi\sim D_{u_{2}}.$ Multiplying ( 6.1 ) 2 by $-\psi_{\xi\xi}$ and integrating over $[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}$ , we have $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}}{2}\,d\xi+\int_{0}^{t}\left.\left(\psi_{t}\psi_{\xi}-\sigma_{-}\frac{|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}}{2}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{|\psi_{\xi}(0,\xi)|^{2}}{2}\,d\xi-\int_{0}^{t}\dot{X}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}u^{S}_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(p-\bar{p})_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mu\left(\frac{u_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q_{1}\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle=:\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{|\psi_{\xi}(0,\xi)|^{2}}{2}\,d\xi+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.$ First, for the boundary terms, we use $\sigma_{-}<0$ and Young’s inequality to have $\left.\left(\psi_{t}\psi_{\xi}-\sigma_{-}\frac{|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}}{2}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\leq\left.\left(C|\psi_{t}|^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{-}}{4}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{-}}{2}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\leq C|\psi_{t}|^{2}\Big|_{\xi=0}.$ Since $|\psi_{t}|\Big|_{\xi=0}\leq|(u^{C}-u_{m})_{t}|\Big|_{\xi=0}+|(u^{S}-u^{*})_{t}|\Big|_{\xi=0},$ and $\displaystyle|(u^{C}-u_{m})_{t}|\Big|_{\xi=0}\leq\left.\Big|\sigma_{-}u_{\xi}^{C}+\mu\left(\frac{u_{\xi}^{C}}{v^{C}}\right)_{\xi}+Q_{1}^{C}\Big|\right|_{\xi=0}\leq C\delta_{C}\left.(1+t)^{-1}e^{-\frac{C_{1}(\xi+\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}}\right|_{\xi=0}$ $\displaystyle\phantom{|(u^{C}-u_{m})_{t}|\Big|_{\xi=0}}\leq C\delta_{C}e^{-Ct},$ $\displaystyle|(u^{S}-u^{*})_{t}|\Big|_{\xi=0}\leq C|(u^{S})^{\prime}(-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)||\dot{X}+(\sigma-\sigma_{-})|\leq C\delta_{S}^{2}e^{-C\delta_{S}((\sigma-\sigma_{-})t+\beta)},$ we obtain (6.7) $C\int_{0}^{t}|\psi_{t}|^{2}\Big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C\big(\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{S}^{3}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}\big).$ For $I_{1}$ , we use Young’s inequality to have $|I_{1}|\leq\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{X}|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{S}||\psi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{S}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{X}|^{2}\,d\tau+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau.$ To estimate $I_{2}$ , note that $\displaystyle|(p-\bar{p})_{\xi}|=R\left|\theta\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}+\frac{1}{\bar{v}}(\theta-\bar{\theta})_{\xi}\right|\leq C\big(|\phi_{\xi}|+|\bar{v}_{\xi}||\phi|+|\chi_{\xi}|\big).$ Using Young’s inequality, we obtain $\displaystyle|I_{2}|=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(p-\bar{p})_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau\right|\leq$ $\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{v}_{\xi}|^{2}|\phi|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau.$ Then, we use Lemma 2.1 , Lemma 2.2 , Lemma 2.4 , and Lemma 2.5 to have $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{v}_{\xi}|^{2}|\phi|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\big(\delta_{BL}G^{BL}+\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}G^{R}+\delta_{S}^{2}G^{S}\big)\,d\tau+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau.$ Thus, we have $\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq$ $\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\big(\delta_{BL}G^{BL}+\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}G^{R}+\delta_{S}^{2}G^{S}+D_{v_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,d\tau+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau.$ For $I_{3}$ , observe that $\displaystyle I_{3}$ $\displaystyle=-\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{1}{v}|\psi_{\xi\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau-\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\frac{1}{v}\right)_{\xi}\psi_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad-\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\bar{u}_{\xi\xi}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau-\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\bar{u}_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big(|\phi_{\xi}|+|\bar{v}_{\xi}|\big)|\psi_{\xi}||\psi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{u}_{\xi\xi}||\phi||\psi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{u}_{\xi}|\big(|\phi_{\xi}|+|\phi||\bar{v}_{\xi}|)|\psi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau=:-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+I_{31}+I_{32}+I_{33}.$ Using the interpolation inequality, we have $\displaystyle|I_{31}|$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\phi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\|\bar{v}_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{3/2}\,d\tau+C(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}+\delta_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon+\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}+\delta_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\big(D_{u_{1}}+D_{u_{2}}\big)\,d\tau.$ Since $|u^{BL}_{\xi\xi}|\leq C|u^{BL}_{\xi}|$ , $|u^{R}_{\xi\xi}|\leq C|u^{R}_{\xi}|$ and $|u^{S}_{\xi\xi}|\leq C|u^{S}_{\xi}|$ , we obtain $\displaystyle|I_{32}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{u}_{\xi\xi}||\phi||\psi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S})\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u^{C}_{\xi\xi}|^{2}|\phi|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S})\,d\tau+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau.$ Next, using Young’s inequality, we have $\displaystyle|I_{33}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{u}_{\xi}|\big(|\phi_{\xi}|+|\phi||\bar{v}_{\xi}|)|\psi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+C(\delta_{0}+\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})\int_{0}^{t}D_{v_{1}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+\delta_{C}B^{C})\,d\tau$ Thus, using $\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}<1$ , we obtain (6.8) $\displaystyle I_{3}$ $\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S})\,d\tau+C(\varepsilon+\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}+\delta_{0})\int_{0}^{t}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{u_{1}})\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau.$ Finally, again we use Lemma 4.3 to have $\displaystyle|I_{4}|=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q_{1}\psi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau\right|\leq$ $\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|Q_{1}|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{u_{2}}\,d\tau.$ Combining all these estimates, we obtain Lemma 6.2 .
∎ 6.3. Higher Order Estimates on $\theta-\bar{\theta}$ Finally, we present the higher order estimates on $\chi$ . Lemma 6.3 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exist positive constants $c$ and $C$ such that $\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\chi_{\xi}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}D_{\theta_{2}}\,dt$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|\chi_{\xi}(0,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{T}(\delta_{S}|\dot{X}|^{2}+G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+D_{v_{1}}+D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}}+D_{u_{2}})\,dt$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{0}^{T}|Q_{2}|^{2}\,dt+Ce^{-c\delta_{S}\beta}.$ Since the proof of this lemma is similar to that of Lemma 6.2 , we provide a sketch of proof. Proof. Throughout the proof of Lemma 6.3 , denote $\mathcal{D}_{\theta_{2}}:=\kappa\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{1}{v}|\chi_{\xi\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi\sim D_{\theta_{2}}.$ Multiplying ( 6.1 ) by $-\chi_{\xi\xi}$ and integrating over $[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{|\chi_{\xi}|^{2}}{2}\,d\xi+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\int_{0}^{t}\left.\left(\chi_{t}\chi_{\xi}-\sigma_{-}\frac{|\chi_{\xi}|^{2}}{2}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad=\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{|\chi_{\xi}(0,\xi)|^{2}}{2}\,d\xi-\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\int_{0}^{t}\dot{X}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\theta_{\xi}^{S}\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(pu_{\xi}-\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi})\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\qquad-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau-\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\frac{u_{\xi}^{2}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q_{2}\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau=:\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{|\chi_{\xi}(0,\xi)|^{2}}{2}\,d\xi+\sum_{i=1}^{5}J_{i}.$ First, for the boundary terms, by following the same argument as in ( 6.7 ) together with $(\theta^{C}-\theta_{m})_{t}\Big|_{\xi=0}\leq\left.\left(\sigma_{-}\theta_{\xi}+C\left(\frac{\theta^{C}_{\xi}}{\theta^{C}}\right)_{\xi}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\leq C\delta_{C}\left.(1+t)^{-1/2}e^{-\frac{C_{1}(\xi+\sigma_{-}t)^{2}}{1+t}}\right|_{\xi=0}\leq C\delta_{C}e^{-Ct},$ we obtain $\int_{0}^{t}\left.\left(\chi_{t}\chi_{\xi}-\sigma_{-}\frac{|\chi_{\xi}|^{2}}{2}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}|\chi_{t}|^{2}\Big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C(\delta_{C}^{2}+\delta_{S}^{3}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}).$ For $J_{1}$ , we use Young’s inequality to have $|J_{1}|\leq C\delta_{S}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{X}|^{2}\,d\tau+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\theta_{2}}\,d\tau.$ To estimate $J_{2}$ , note that $|pu_{\xi}-\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi}|\leq|p\psi_{\xi}|+|\bar{u}_{\xi}(p-\bar{p})|\leq C|\psi_{\xi}|+C|\bar{u}_{\xi}|(|\phi|+|\chi|).$ Therefore, we have $\displaystyle|J_{2}|=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(pu_{\xi}-\bar{p}\bar{u}_{\xi})\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau\right|$ $\displaystyle\,\,\leq\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\theta_{2}}d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}d\tau+C(\delta_{0}+\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S})d\tau+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}d\tau.$ Now, for $J_{3}$ , observe that $\displaystyle J_{3}$ $\displaystyle=-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{1}{v}|\chi_{\xi\xi}|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\frac{1}{v}\right)_{\xi}\chi_{\xi}\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad\quad-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\bar{\theta}_{\xi\xi}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\bar{\theta}_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)\chi_{\xi\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau.$ In a similar way to the estimate of $I_{3}$ in ( 6.8 ), we derive $\displaystyle J_{3}$ $\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\theta_{2}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S})\,d\tau+C(\varepsilon+\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}+\delta_{0})\int_{0}^{t}(D_{v}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau.$ To estimate $J_{4}$ , note that $\displaystyle\frac{u_{\xi}^{2}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}=\frac{1}{v}(u_{\xi}^{2}-\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2})+\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v}-\frac{1}{\bar{v}}\right)\leq C(|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}+|\psi_{\xi}||\bar{u}_{\xi}|)+C\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}|\phi|.$ This yields $\displaystyle|J_{4}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}+|\psi_{\xi}||\bar{u}_{\xi}|)|\chi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\bar{u}_{\xi}|^{2}|\phi||\chi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\theta_{2}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}|\chi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C(\delta_{0}+\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S})\,d\tau+C\delta_{C}^{3}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau.$ On the other hand, using the interpolation inequality and Young’s inequality, we find that $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\psi_{\xi}|^{2}|\chi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\|\chi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\,d\tau\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{3/2}\|\psi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|\chi_{\xi\xi}\|_{L^{2}}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{4}\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{2}}\,d\tau+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\theta_{2}}\,d\tau.$ Thus, we obtain $\displaystyle J_{4}$ $\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{50}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\theta_{2}}\,d\tau+C(\delta_{0}+\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})\int_{0}^{t}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S})\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}D_{{u_{2}}}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\delta_{C}^{3}\int_{0}^{t}B^{C}\,d\tau.$ Finally, applying Lemma 4.3 , we have $\displaystyle|J_{5}|\leq\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|Q_{2}||\chi_{\xi\xi}|\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq$ $\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|Q_{2}|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau+\frac{1}{100}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}_{\theta_{2}}\,d\tau.$ By combining all these estimates, we obtain the desired estimates in Lemma 6.3 .
∎ Lemma 6.4 . Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.2 , there exists a positive constant $C$ such that, for any $0<t<T$ , $\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big(|Q_{1}|^{2}+|Q_{2}|^{2}\big)\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{0}+C(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{2}.$ Proof. Recall from ( 5.2 ) that $\sum_{i=1}^{2}Q_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{2}(Q_{i}^{I}+Q_{i}^{R}+Q_{i}^{C}),$ where $Q_{i}^{I},Q_{i}^{R}$ , and $Q_{i}^{C}$ are defined in ( 5.3 ), ( 5.4 ), and ( 2.7 ).
Using ( 5.42 ), ( 5.43 ), ( 4.16 ), and ( 4.24 ), we have $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|Q_{1}^{I}|^{2}+|Q_{2}^{I}|^{2})d\xi d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{6}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|J_{i}|^{2}d\xi d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(u_{\xi}^{C},u_{\xi\xi}^{C})|^{2}|\bar{v}-v^{C}|^{2}d\xi d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad+C\sum_{i\neq j}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|(u_{i})_{\xi}|^{2}(|(v_{j})_{\xi}|+|(u_{j})_{\xi}|)^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{0}+C(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{2}+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{2}.$ Next, from ( 5.44 ) and Lemma 2.3 , we have $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big(|Q_{1}^{R}|^{2}+|Q_{2}^{R}|^{2}\big)\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|u_{\xi\xi}^{R}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u_{\xi}^{R}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\big)\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|u_{\xi\xi}^{R}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\|u_{\xi\xi}^{R}\|_{L^{2}}^{3/2}+\|u_{\xi}^{R}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|u_{\xi}^{R}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\big)\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C\delta_{R}^{2}\varepsilon_{r}((\delta_{R})^{1/2}+(\delta_{R})^{1/8})^{3/2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{-21/16}\,d\tau+C(\delta_{R}^{2}\varepsilon_{r}^{3/2})\delta_{R}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{-3/2}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C\delta_{R}^{2}\varepsilon_{r}((\delta_{R})^{1/2}+(\delta_{R})^{1/8})^{3/2}+C(\delta_{R}^{2}\varepsilon_{r}^{3/2})\delta_{R}^{1/2}\leq C\delta_{R}^{5/2}\varepsilon_{r}.$ Here, we take $q=8$ in Lemma 2.3 .
Finally, by ( 2.9 ), we have $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big(|Q_{1}^{C}|^{2}+|Q_{2}^{C}|^{2}\big)\,d\tau\leq C\delta_{C}^{2}.$ Combining all these estimates and using the smallness of $\delta_{0}$ and $\varepsilon_{r}$ , we obtain the desired estimate in Lemma 6.4 .
∎ $\bullet$ Proof of Proposition 3.2 : Combining Lemma 6.1 – 6.4 , and choosing $\nu>0$ sufficiently small in Lemma 6.1 , we obtain $\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(\phi_{\xi},\psi_{\xi},\chi_{\xi})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{u_{2}}+D_{\theta_{2}})\,dt$ $\displaystyle\leq C\|(\psi,\phi_{\xi},\psi_{\xi},\chi_{\xi})(0,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{T}(\delta_{S}|\dot{X}|^{2}+G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}})\,dt$ $\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{T}B^{C}\,dt++C\left(\delta_{0}+(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})+\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{2}\right)+Ce^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}.$ Applying Lemma 5.10 and then combining the above estimates with Lemma 5.1 , we obtain the desired estimate in Proposition 3.2 by choosing $\varepsilon_{r}:=\delta_{0}^{1/4}$ as defined in ( 5.50 ). ∎ Appendix A Proof of Lemma 5.10 In this section, we provide a sketch of the proof of Lemma 5.10 . It suffices to establish the following estimate: (A.1) $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(1+\tau)}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\frac{C_{1}|\xi+\sigma_{-}\tau|^{2}}{1+\tau}}\left(\left(\bar{p}\phi+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\chi\right)^{2}+\frac{(R\chi-\bar{p}\phi)^{2}}{2v}+\frac{\gamma\bar{p}}{2}\psi^{2}\right)d\xi d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\sup\limits_{\tau\in\left[0,T\right]}||(U-\overline{U})(\tau,\cdot)||^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+C\delta_{S}\int_{0}^{t}|\dot{X}(\tau)|^{2}d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}(G^{S}+G^{R}+G^{BL})d\tau$ $\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}(D_{v_{1}}+D_{u_{1}}+D_{\theta_{1}}+D_{u_{2}}+D_{\theta_{2}})d\tau+C.$ Indeed, there exists $\widetilde{C}>0$ such that $(\phi^{2}+\psi^{2}+\chi^{2})\leq\widetilde{C}\left(\left(\bar{p}\phi+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\chi\right)^{2}+\frac{(R\chi-\bar{p}\phi)^{2}}{2v}+\frac{\gamma\bar{p}}{2}\psi^{2}\right).$ Combining this inequality with ( A.1 ) directly yields the desired estimate in Lemma 5.10 . Thus, it remains to prove ( A.1 ). For simplicity, we introduce the following notation. Unless otherwise specified, all functions below are defined on $\mathbb{R}_{+}$ , whereas $\mathcal{H}$ is defined on the whole line $\mathbb{R}$ . $\displaystyle P:=\bar{p}\phi+\frac{R}{\gamma-1}\chi,$ $\displaystyle\widetilde{P}=R\chi-\bar{p}\phi,$ $\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(t,\xi):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+t}}e^{-\frac{b|\xi+\sigma_{-}t|^{2}}{1+t}},$ $\displaystyle b:=\frac{C_{1}}{2},$ $\displaystyle H_{1}(t,\xi):=\int_{-\infty}^{\xi}\mathcal{H}(t,y)\,dy,$ $\displaystyle H_{2}(t,\xi):=\int_{-\infty}^{\xi}|\mathcal{H}(t,y)|^{2}\,dy.$ Note that $H_{1}$ satisfies $(H_{1})_{t}-\sigma_{-}(H_{1})_{\xi}=\frac{1}{4b}(H_{1})_{\xi\xi}$ , and (A.2) $|H_{1}(t,\xi)|\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\mathcal{H}(t,\xi)|\,d\xi\leq\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{b}},\quad|H_{2}(t,\xi)|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{1+t}},\quad|(H_{2})_{t}-\sigma_{-}(H_{2})_{\xi}|\leq\frac{C}{(1+t)^{\frac{3}{2}}}.$ We follow the same calculations in [ 29 , Appendix D] to have $\displaystyle\frac{1}{4b}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}P^{2}\mathcal{H}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{P(0,\xi)^{2}H_{1}(0,\xi)^{2}}{2}\,d\xi-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{P^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2}\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\dot{X}\Big(\bar{p}v_{\xi}^{S}+\frac{R\theta_{\xi}^{S}}{\gamma-1}\Big)PH_{1}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau-\frac{1}{4b}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(P^{2})_{\xi}H_{1}\mathcal{H}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(p-\bar{p})\psi_{\xi}PH_{1}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left((\bar{p}_{t}-\sigma_{-}\bar{p}_{\xi})\phi-(p-\bar{p})\bar{u}_{\xi}+\mu\left(\frac{u_{\xi}^{2}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)-Q_{2}\right)PH_{1}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\kappa\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}PH_{1}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\left.\left(\sigma_{-}\frac{P^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2}-\frac{1}{4b}P^{2}H_{1}\mathcal{H}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}=:\sum_{i=1}^{8}Z_{i}.$ Since the estimates of $Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{4}$ are essentially the same as those in [ 29 ] , we omit the details and focus on the remaining terms. First, we decompose $Z_{5}$ as $\displaystyle Z_{5}$ $\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\frac{\gamma\bar{p}}{2v}\phi^{2}PH_{1}^{2}-\frac{(\gamma-1)}{v}\phi P^{2}H_{1}^{2}\right)_{t}-\sigma_{-}\left(\frac{\gamma\bar{p}}{2v}\phi^{2}PH_{1}^{2}-\frac{(\gamma-1)}{v}\phi P^{2}H_{1}^{2}\right)_{\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\frac{\gamma\bar{p}}{v}\phi^{2}P-\frac{2(\gamma-1)}{v}\phi P^{2}\right)H_{1}[(H_{1})_{t}-\sigma_{-}(H_{1})_{\xi}]\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left((\partial_{t}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi})\frac{\gamma\bar{p}}{v}P\right)\frac{\phi^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left((\partial_{t}-\sigma_{-}\partial_{\xi})\frac{(\gamma-1)}{v}P^{2}\right)\phi H_{1}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\dot{X}\frac{(\bar{p}\phi-R\chi)}{v}v_{\xi}^{S}PH_{1}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau=:\sum_{i=1}^{5}Z_{5,i}.$ Since the estimates of $Z_{5,2},Z_{5,5}$ are essentially same as in [ 29 ] , we omit the details and focus on the remaining terms. For $Z_{5,1}$ , we have $|Z_{5,1}|\leq C\sup_{\tau\in[0,T]}\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\tau\leq C\sup_{\tau\in[0,T]}\|(\phi,\chi)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\delta_{C}^{2}+e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}).$ We further decompose $Z_{5,3}$ as $\displaystyle Z_{5,3}$ $\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\dot{X}\left(v_{\xi}^{S}\bar{p}+\frac{R\theta_{\xi}^{S}}{\gamma-1}\right)\frac{\gamma\bar{p}\phi^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2v}d\xi d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\kappa\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)\left(\frac{\gamma\bar{p}\phi^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2v}\right)_{\xi}d\xi d\tau$ $\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(-(p-\bar{p})\psi_{\xi}-(p-\bar{p})\bar{u}_{\xi}+\mu\left(\frac{u^{2}_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)-Q_{2}\right)\frac{\gamma\bar{p}\phi^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2v}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left((\bar{p}_{t}-\sigma_{-}\bar{p}_{\xi})\frac{\gamma\bar{p}\phi^{3}H_{1}^{2}}{2v}+\left(\left(\frac{\bar{p}}{v}\right)_{t}-\sigma_{-}\left(\frac{\bar{p}}{v}\right)_{\xi}\right)\frac{\gamma P\phi^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2}\right)\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\kappa\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)\left(\frac{\gamma\bar{p}\phi^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2v}\right)\Big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau.$ Except for the term containing $Q_{2}$ and the boundary term, the remaining terms can be treated similarly to those in [ 29 ] . Hence, we only present the estimates for these two terms and omit the details of the others. Following the arguments in estimating $B^{I}_{1}$ and $B^{I}_{2}$ in Lemma 5.11 , and using ( 5.50 ), we obtain $\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q_{2}\frac{\gamma\bar{p}\phi^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2v}\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Q_{2}|\phi|\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}D_{v_{1}}\,d\tau+C.$ Moreover, similar to the estimate of $J_{2}^{bd}$ and $J_{3}^{bd}$ in Lemma 5.12 , we have (A.3) $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\kappa\left.\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)\left(\frac{\gamma\bar{p}\phi^{2}H_{1}^{2}}{2v}\right)\right|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\left|\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right||\phi|\Big|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{C}+\delta_{S}^{1/3}e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta})+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{t}D_{\theta_{2}}\,d\tau.$ Since $Z_{5,4}$ and $Z_{6}$ can be estimated similarly to $Z_{5,3}$ , we omit the details. To estimate $Z_{7}$ , integration by parts yields $Z_{7}=-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\left.\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)PH_{1}^{2}\right|_{\xi=0}\,d\tau-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)(PH_{1}^{2})_{\xi}\,d\xi\,d\tau=:Z_{7,1}+Z_{7,2}.$ The term $Z_{7,1}$ can be estimated similarly to ( A.3 ), so we omit the details. Next, following the arguments in [ 29 ] , for any sufficiently small $k_{1}>0$ , we obtain $\displaystyle|Z_{7,2}|$ $\displaystyle\leq(k_{1}+C\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r}+C\delta_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\mathcal{H}|^{2}|(\phi,\chi)|^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau+C_{k_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}D_{\theta_{1}}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(G^{BL}+G^{R}+G^{S}+D_{v_{1}})\,d\xi\,d\tau.$ Finally, $Z_{8}$ can be estimated similarly to $Z_{5,1}$ , and hence we omit the details. Therefore, we obtain the desired estimate for $\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}P^{2}\mathcal{H}^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ . It remains to estimate the terms involving $\widetilde{P}$ and $\psi$ . For this purpose, we use the following identity (see [ 29 ] for the details): $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{\mathcal{H}^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{|\widetilde{P}|^{2}}{v}+\gamma\bar{p}\psi^{2}\right)d\xi d\tau=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi H_{2}\widetilde{P}\Bigg|_{\tau=0}^{\tau=t}d\xi-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{H_{2}|\widetilde{P}|^{2}}{2v^{2}}v_{\xi}d\xi d\tau\\
&-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi\widetilde{P}\left[(H_{2})_{t}-\sigma_{-}(H_{2})_{\xi}\right]d\xi d\tau-\frac{\gamma}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\bar{p}_{\xi}\psi^{2}H_{2}d\xi d\tau\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\dot{X}(t)H_{2}\left(\bar{p}\psi v^{S}_{\xi}-R(\gamma-1)\psi\theta^{S}_{\xi}-\widetilde{P}u^{S}_{\xi}\right)d\xi d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi H_{2}\phi(\bar{p}_{t}-\sigma_{-}\bar{p}_{\xi})d\xi d\tau\\
&+(\gamma-1)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi H_{2}\left[(p-\bar{p})\psi_{\xi}+(p-\bar{p})\bar{u}_{\xi}-\kappa\left(\frac{\theta_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{\theta}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}-\mu\left(\frac{u_{\xi}^{2}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}^{2}}{\bar{v}}\right)+Q_{2}\right]d\xi d\tau\\
&-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}H_{2}\widetilde{P}\left[\mu\left(\frac{u_{\xi}}{v}-\frac{\bar{u}_{\xi}}{\bar{v}}\right)_{\xi}-Q_{1}\right]d\xi d\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{H_{2}|\widetilde{P}|^{2}}{2v}-\sigma_{-}\psi H_{2}\widetilde{P}+\frac{\gamma\psi^{2}H_{2}\bar{p}}{2}\right)\Bigg|_{\xi=0}d\tau\\
&=:\sum\limits_{i=1}^{9}\mathcal{Z}_{i}.\end{aligned}$ Since the estimates of $\mathcal{Z}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{Z}_{9}$ , except for $\mathcal{Z}_{4}$ are similar to those for $Z_{i}$ , we omit the details and only provide the estimate for $\mathcal{Z}_{4}$ . We remark that, by estimating $\mathcal{Z}_{8}$ in the same manner as $Z_{7}$ , one obtains the term $D_{u_{2}}$ in ( A.1 ). First, note that $\displaystyle|\mathcal{Z}_{4}|$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|v^{BL}_{\xi}|+|v^{R}_{\xi}|+|v^{C}_{\xi}|+|v^{S}_{\xi}|)\psi^{2}H_{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(|v^{BL}_{\xi}|+|v^{R}_{\xi}|)\psi^{2}H_{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}G^{S}\,d\tau+C\delta_{C}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathcal{H}^{2}\psi^{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau.$ Therefore, it suffices to estimate the first term on the right-hand side of the above inequality. Using Young’s inequality, ( A.2 ), ( 6.6 ), and Lemma 2.1 , we obtain $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{BL}|\psi^{2}H_{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi^{2}\big(|v_{\xi}^{BL}|^{5/3}+|H_{2}|^{5/2}\big)\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(|\psi(0,\xi)|+\sqrt{\xi}||\psi_{\xi}||_{L^{2}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\delta_{BL}}{1+\delta_{BL}\xi}\right)^{\frac{10}{3}}d\xi d\tau+C\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(1+\tau)^{\frac{5}{4}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi^{2}d\xi d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(|\psi(0,\xi)|^{2}+\|\psi_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{\delta_{BL}^{\frac{7}{3}}(1+\delta_{BL}\xi)}{\left(1+\delta_{BL}\xi\right)^{\frac{10}{3}}}d\xi d\tau+C\varepsilon^{2}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{BL}^{4/3}\left(\int_{0}^{t}D_{u_{1}}\,d\tau+\delta_{C}^{2}+e^{-C\delta_{S}\beta}\right)+C\varepsilon^{2}.$ On the other hand, using ( A.2 ), Hölder’s inequality, ( 3.4 ) and Lemma 2.2 , we obtain $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{R}|\psi^{2}H_{2}\,d\xi\,d\tau\leq\int_{0}^{t}\frac{C}{\sqrt{1+\tau}}\|v_{\xi}^{R}\|_{L^{4}}\|\psi^{2}\|_{L^{4/3}}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\frac{C}{\sqrt{1+\tau}}\frac{\delta_{R}^{1/4}}{(1+\tau)^{3/4}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\psi|^{8/3}\,d\xi\right)^{3/4}\,d\tau\leq\int_{0}^{t}\frac{C\delta_{R}^{1/4}}{(1+\tau)^{5/4}}\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/2}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{3/2}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{R}^{1/4}\varepsilon^{2}.$ Therefore, combining the above estimates, we obtain the desired estimate for $\mathcal{Z}_{4}$ . This completes the proof of ( A.1 ), and hence of Lemma 5.10 . Appendix B Proof of Lemma 4.2 In this section, we provide a proof of Lemma 4.2 . $\bullet$ Proof of ( 4.12 ): First, observe that $|u_{\xi}^{C}||\bar{v}-v^{C}|=|u_{\xi}^{C}|\big(|v^{BL}-v_{*}|+|v^{R}-v_{m}|+|v^{S}-v^{*}|\big).$ Following the calculations in Lemma 4.1 , and using Lemma 2.1 –Lemma 2.4 , we obtain $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{BL}-v_{*}|\,d\xi&=\left(\int_{0}^{-\frac{\sigma_{-}t}{2}}+\int_{-\frac{\sigma_{-}t}{2}}^{\infty}\right)|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{BL}-v_{*}|\,d\xi\\
&\leq C\delta_{BL}\delta_{C}\left(e^{-Ct}+\frac{\ln(1+\delta_{BL}t)}{1+t}\right),\end{aligned}$ $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{R}-v_{m}|\,d\xi\\
&\quad\leq\left(\int_{0\leq\xi\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}(u_{m},\theta_{m})(1+t)-\sigma_{-}t}+\int_{\xi\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}(u_{m},\theta_{m})(1+t)-\sigma_{-}t}\right)|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{R}-v_{m}|\,d\xi\\
&\quad\leq C\delta_{C}\delta_{R}e^{-Ct}+C\delta_{C}\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}e^{-C\varepsilon_{r}t}.\end{aligned}$ From Hölder’s inequality, we find that $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{S}-v^{*}|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}|\,d\xi\right)^{3/4}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{S}-v^{*}|^{4}\,d\xi\right)^{1/4}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{C}^{3/4}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{S}-v^{*}|^{4}\,d\xi\right)^{1/4}.$ Using Lemma 2.5 , we obtain $\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{S}-v^{*}|^{4}\,d\xi=\left(\int_{0}^{\alpha t}+\int_{\alpha t}^{\infty}\right)|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{S}-v^{*}|^{4}\,d\xi\leq C\delta_{C}\delta_{S}^{3}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+C\delta_{C}\delta_{S}^{4}e^{-Ct},$ where $\alpha=\frac{\sigma-2\sigma_{-}}{2}$ . Therefore, we have $\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}||v^{S}-v^{*}|\,d\xi\leq C\delta_{C}\big(\delta_{S}^{3/4}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+\delta_{S}e^{-Ct}\big).$ Thus, we obtain (B.1) $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi}^{C}||\bar{v}-v^{C}|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{C}\left[\delta_{BL}\left(e^{-Ct}+\frac{\ln(1+\delta_{BL}t)}{(1+t)}\right)+(\delta_{R}+\delta_{S})e^{-Ct}+\delta_{S}^{3/4}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}e^{-C\varepsilon_{r}t}\right].$ Following the same argument in deriving ( B.1 ), we have $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|u_{\xi\xi}^{C}||\bar{v}-v^{C}|d\xi$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C\delta_{C}\left[\delta_{BL}\left(e^{-Ct}+\frac{\ln(1+\delta_{BL}t)}{(1+t)}\right)+(\delta_{R}+\delta_{S})e^{-Ct}+\delta_{S}^{3/4}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}e^{-C\varepsilon_{r}t}\right].$ $\bullet$ Proof of ( 4.13 ): Let $b:=\frac{\sigma-\sigma_{-}}{2}>0$ and $\alpha=\frac{\sigma-2\sigma_{-}}{2}$ . From Lemma 2.1 – 2.5 and ( 4.4 ), we obtain the followings: $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{BL}||v_{\xi}^{R}|\,d\xi\leq$ $\displaystyle C\frac{(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{1/8}}{(1+t)^{7/8}}\delta_{BL}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{1}{(1+\delta_{BL}\xi)^{2}}\,d\xi\leq C\delta_{BL}\frac{(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{1/8}}{(1+t)^{7/8}},$ $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{BL}||v_{\xi}^{S}|\,d\xi\leq$ $\displaystyle C{\delta_{BL}^{2}}\delta_{S}^{2}\left(\int_{0}^{bt}+\int_{bt}^{\infty}\right)\frac{e^{-C\delta_{S}|\xi-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta|}}{(1+{\delta_{BL}}\xi)^{2}}d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq$ $\displaystyle C\delta_{BL}\delta_{S}\left(\delta_{BL}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+\frac{\delta_{S}}{1+\delta_{BL}t}\right),$ $\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{R}||v_{\xi}^{S}|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0\leq\xi\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}(u_{m},\theta_{m})(1+t)-\sigma_{-}t}+\int_{\xi\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}(u_{m},\theta_{m})(1+t)-\sigma_{-}t}\right)|v_{\xi}^{R}||v_{\xi}^{S}|\,d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})\delta_{S}e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+C\delta_{S}^{2}\frac{(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})}{\varepsilon_{r}}e^{-C\varepsilon_{r}t}.$ Moreover, by calculations similar to those in ( 4.2 ), we obtain $\begin{split}&\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{BL}|\big(|v_{\xi}^{C}|+|u_{\xi}^{C}|\big)\,d\xi\leq\left(\int_{0}^{-\frac{\sigma_{-}t}{2}}+\int_{-\frac{\sigma_{-}t}{2}}^{\infty}\right)|v_{\xi}^{BL}|\big(|v_{\xi}^{C}|+|u_{\xi}^{C}|\big)\,d\xi\\
&\phantom{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{BL}|\big(|v_{\xi}^{C}|+|u_{\xi}^{C}|\big)\,d\xi}\leq C\delta_{BL}\delta_{C}\left(e^{-Ct}+\frac{1}{1+\delta_{BL}t}\right),\end{aligned}\\
&\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{R}|\big(|v_{\xi}^{C}|+|u_{\xi}^{C}|\big)\,d\xi\\
&\quad\leq\left(\int_{0\leq\xi\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}(u_{m},\theta_{m})(1+t)-\sigma_{-}t}+\int_{\xi\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}(u_{m},\theta_{m})(1+t)-\sigma_{-}t}\right)|v_{\xi}^{R}|\big(|v_{\xi}^{C}|+|u_{\xi}^{C}|\big)\,d\xi\\
&\quad\leq C\delta_{C}\left((\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})e^{-Ct}+\frac{(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})}{\varepsilon_{r}}e^{-C\varepsilon_{r}t}\right),\end{aligned}\end{split}$ $\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|v_{\xi}^{S}|\big(|v_{\xi}^{C}|+|u_{\xi}^{C}|\big)\,d\xi\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\alpha t}+\int_{\alpha t}^{\infty}\right)|v_{\xi}^{S}|\big(|v_{\xi}^{C}|+|u_{\xi}^{C}|\big)\,d\xi\leq C\delta_{S}\delta_{C}\left(e^{-C\delta_{S}t}+e^{-Ct}\right).$ $\bullet$ Proof of ( 4.14 ): Combining ( 4.12 ) and ( 4.13 ), we obtain $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\||(u_{\xi}^{C},u_{\xi\xi}^{C})||\bar{v}-v^{C}|\|_{L^{1}}^{4/3}+\sum_{i\neq j}\||(v_{i})_{\xi}|(|(v_{j})_{\xi}|+|(u_{j})_{\xi}|)\|_{L^{1}}^{4/3}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq C\delta_{C}^{4/3}\left(\delta_{BL}^{4/3}+\delta_{R}^{4/3}+\delta_{S}^{4/3}+1+\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\delta_{BL}^{4/3}(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{1/6}+C\big(\delta_{BL}^{4/3}\delta_{C}^{4/3}+\delta_{BL}^{1/3}\delta_{C}^{4/3}\big)+C\big(\delta_{BL}^{8/3}\delta_{S}^{1/3}+\delta_{BL}^{1/3}\delta_{S}^{8/3}\big)$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\left(\delta_{C}^{4/3}(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{4/3}+\delta_{C}^{4/3}\left(\frac{(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)$ $\displaystyle\qquad+C\left((\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{4/3}\delta_{S}^{1/3}+\delta_{S}^{8/3}\left(\frac{(\varepsilon_{r}\delta_{R})}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)+C\delta_{S}^{4/3}\delta_{C}^{4/3}.$ Thanks to the smallness of $\delta_{0}$ and $\varepsilon_{r}$ , we obtain $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\||(u_{\xi}^{C},u_{\xi\xi}^{C})||\bar{v}-v^{C}|\|_{L^{1}}^{4/3}+\sum_{i\neq j}\||(v_{i})_{\xi}|(|(v_{j})_{\xi}|+|(u_{j})_{\xi}|)\|_{L^{1}}^{4/3}\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\delta_{0}\big(1+(\delta_{R}\varepsilon_{r})^{1/6}\big)+C\delta_{0}\left(\frac{\delta_{R}}{\varepsilon_{r}}\right)^{4/3}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{r}}.$ ∎ Appendix C Proof of Global Existence In this section, we prove the global existence of ( 1.3 ). Assume that the constants $\delta_{0},\varepsilon,c,C_{0}$ , and $\beta$ from Proposition 3.2 are given. First, we choose smooth functions $\hat{v}$ , $\hat{u}$ , and $\hat{\theta}$ defined on $\mathbb{R}_{+}$ such that (C.1) $\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|(\hat{v}-v^{*},\hat{u}-u^{*},\hat{\theta}-\theta^{*})\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}+\|(\hat{v}-v_{+},\hat{u}-u_{+},\hat{\theta}-\theta_{+})\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}\\
&\quad+\|(\hat{v}_{\xi},\hat{u}_{\xi},\hat{\theta}_{\xi})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq\hat{C}_{0}\delta_{0},\end{aligned}$ for some constant $\hat{C}_{0}>0$ . Define the smooth function $\underline{U}:=(\underline{v},\underline{u},\underline{\theta})$ as $\underline{U}(\xi):=U^{R}(0,\xi)+U^{C}(0,\xi)+\hat{U}(\xi)-U_{m}-U^{*}.$ Then we have (C.2) $\displaystyle\|\underline{v}(\cdot)-\bar{v}(0,\cdot)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}=\|(v^{BL}(\cdot)+v^{S}(\cdot-\beta)-\hat{v}(\cdot)-v_{\ast})\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}$ $\displaystyle\leq\|v^{BL}-v_{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|\hat{v}-v^{C}(0,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}+\|v^{S}(\cdot-\beta)-v^{*}\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}$ $\displaystyle+\|\hat{v}-v_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}+\|v^{S}(\cdot-\beta)-v_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}+\|(\hat{v}_{\xi},v^{BL}_{\xi})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|v^{S}_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}$ $\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{1}\sqrt{\delta_{0}},$ for some constant $\hat{C}_{1}>0$ . By the same argument, after retaking $\hat{C}_{1}$ if necessary, we obtain $\|(\underline{v}-\bar{v},\underline{u}-\bar{u},\underline{\theta}-\bar{\theta})(0,\cdot)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq\hat{C}_{1}\sqrt{\delta_{0}}.$ For a given $\varepsilon_{0}>0$ , we choose $\delta_{0},\delta_{S},$ sufficiently small and $\beta>0$ sufficiently large such that $\frac{\varepsilon}{4(C_{0}+1)}-\hat{C}_{0}\delta_{0}-\hat{C}_{1}\sqrt{\delta_{0}}-\delta_{0}^{1/40}-e^{-c\delta_{S}\beta}>0$ holds. We now introduce two constants, $\varepsilon_{0}$ and $\varepsilon_{*}$ , defined by $\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{\varepsilon}{4(C_{0}+1)},\qquad\varepsilon_{*}:=\frac{\varepsilon}{2(C_{0}+1)}-\hat{C}_{1}\sqrt{\delta_{0}}-\delta_{0}^{1/40}-e^{-c\delta_{S}\beta},$ where $\hat{C}_{0}$ is the constant in ( C.1 ). Now, consider any initial data $(v_{0},u_{0},\theta_{0})$ satisfying ( 1.19 ), that is, (C.3) $\displaystyle\|U_{0}-U^{*}-(U^{R}(0,\cdot)-U_{m})\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}+\|U_{0}-U_{+}-(U^{R}(0,\cdot)-U_{m})\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}$ $\displaystyle+\|(U_{0}-U^{R}(0,\cdot))_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}<\varepsilon_{0}.$ From ( C.1 ) and ( C.3 ), we obtain (C.4) $\displaystyle\|U_{0}-\underline{U}\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}$ $\displaystyle\leq\|(U_{0}-U^{*}-(U^{R}(0,\cdot)-U_{m})\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}+\|U^{C}(0,\cdot)-U^{*}\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}+\|\hat{U}-U^{*}\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}$ $\displaystyle+\|U_{0}-U_{+}-(U^{R}(0,\cdot)-U_{m})\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}+\|U^{C}(0,\cdot)-U^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}+\|\hat{U}-U_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}$ $\displaystyle+\|(U_{0}-U^{R}(0,\cdot))_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|(\hat{U}_{\xi},U^{C}_{\xi})(0,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq\varepsilon_{0}+\hat{C}_{0}\delta_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{*}.$ In particular, Sobolev embedding implies $\|(v_{0}-\underline{v},\theta_{0}-\underline{\theta})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}<C\varepsilon_{*}$ . Thus, by choosing $\varepsilon_{*}$ sufficiently small, we obtain $\frac{\min\{v_{-},v_{+}\}}{2}<v_{0}(x)<2\max\{v_{-},v_{+}\},\,\,\frac{\min\{\theta_{-},\theta_{+}\}}{2}<\theta_{0}(x)<2\max\{\theta_{-},\theta_{+}\},\,\,\xi\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.$ Hence, Proposition 3.1 implies that there exists $T_{0}>0$ such that the system ( 1.2 ) admits a unique solution $(v,u,\theta)$ on $[0,T_{0}]$ satisfying (C.5) $\|v-\underline{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}))}+\|u-\underline{u}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}))}+\|\theta-\underline{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{0};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}))}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},$ and for any $t\in[0,T_{0}]$ and $\xi\in\mathbb{R}_{+}$ , $\frac{\min\{v_{-},v_{+}\}}{4}<v(t,x)<4\max\{v_{-},v_{+}\},\,\,\frac{\min\{\theta_{-},\theta_{+}\}}{4}<\theta(t,x)<4\max\{\theta_{-},\theta_{+}\}.$ On the other hand, observe that (C.6) $\displaystyle\|(\underline{U}-\overline{U})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}$ $\displaystyle\leq\|U^{BL}-U_{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|U^{R}(0,\cdot)-U^{R}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|U^{C}(0,\cdot)-U^{C}(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}$ $\displaystyle+\|\hat{U}-U^{*}\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}+\|U^{S}(\cdot-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)-U^{*}\|_{L^{2}(0,\beta)}$ $\displaystyle+\|\hat{U}-U_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}+\|U^{S}(\cdot-(\sigma-\sigma_{-})t-X(t)-\beta)-U_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\beta,\infty)}$ $\displaystyle+\|(\hat{U}_{\xi},U^{BL}_{\xi},U^{R}_{\xi}(0,\cdot),U^{R}_{\xi}(t,\cdot),U^{C}_{\xi}(0,\cdot),U^{C}_{\xi}(t,\cdot))\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|U^{S}_{\xi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}$ $\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{t}(\sqrt{\ln(1+t)}+(1+t)^{1/4})+C\sqrt{\delta_{0}}(1+\sqrt{|X(t)|}+\sqrt{t})$ $\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{t}(\sqrt{\ln(1+t)}+(1+t)^{1/4})+C\sqrt{\delta_{0}}(1+\sqrt{t}).$ Here, the first term of the last inequality is obtained as follows: using Hölder’s inequality, ( 2.4 ) 1 , and Lemma 2.3 , we have $\displaystyle\|v^{R}(t,\cdot)-v^{R}(0,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\int_{0}^{t}v^{R}_{\tau}(\tau,\xi)d\tau\right|^{2}d\xi\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}t\int_{0}^{t}|v^{R}_{\tau}(\tau,\xi)|^{2}d\tau d\xi$ $\displaystyle\quad\leq Ct\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(|v^{R}_{\xi}(\tau,\xi)|^{2}+|u^{R}_{\xi}(\tau,\xi)|^{2}\right)d\xi d\tau\leq Ct\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\tau+1}d\tau\leq Ct\ln(1+t),$ and using ( 1.16 ) 1 and Lemma 2.4 , $\displaystyle\|v^{C}(t,\cdot)-v^{C}(0,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\int_{0}^{t}v^{C}_{\tau}(\tau,\xi)d\tau\right|^{2}d\xi$ $\displaystyle\leq Ct\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(|v^{C}_{\xi}(\tau,\xi)|^{2}+|u^{C}_{\xi}(\tau,\xi)|^{2}\right)d\xi d\tau\leq Ct\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\delta_{C}^{2}}{1+\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\frac{C_{1}(\xi+\sigma_{-}\tau)^{2}}{1+\tau}}\,d\xi\,d\tau$ $\displaystyle\leq C\delta_{C}^{2}t\sqrt{1+t}.$ Similarly, we can obtain the same estimates for $u^{R}$ , $\theta^{R}$ , $u^{C}$ , and $\theta^{C}$ . For the details of the remaining terms in ( C.6 ), we refer to [ 28 ] . Therefore, if we choose $\delta_{0}$ and $T_{1}\in(0,T_{0})$ small enough so that $C\sqrt{T_{1}}(\sqrt{\ln(1+T_{1})}+(1+T_{1})^{1/4})+C\sqrt{\delta}(1+\sqrt{T_{1}})<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}$ , we have $\|(\underline{v}-\bar{v},\underline{u}-\bar{u},\underline{\theta}-\bar{\theta})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{1};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}))}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.$ Combining with the estimate ( C.5 ), we obtain $\|(v-\bar{v},u-\bar{u},\theta-\bar{\theta})(t,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T_{1};H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}))}\leq\varepsilon.$ Moreover, since it holds that $(v-\underline{v},u-\underline{u},\theta-\underline{\theta})\in C([0,T_{1}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})),$ and the shift $X(t)$ is Lipschitz continuous, we obtain $(v-\bar{v},u-\bar{u},\theta-\bar{\theta})\in C([0,T_{1}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}))$ . We now show that the solution can be globally extended by using the standard continuation argument. To this end, we define the maximal existence time $T_{M}:=\sup\left\{T>0~\Bigg|~\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(v-\bar{v},u-\bar{u},\theta-\bar{\theta})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\leq\varepsilon\right\}.$ Suppose, for the contradiction, that $T_{M}$ is finite. Then, by the definition of $T_{M}$ , we have $\sup_{t\in[0,T_{M}]}\|(v-\bar{v},u-\bar{u},\theta-\bar{\theta})(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}=\varepsilon.$ On the other hand, using ( C.2 ) and ( C.4 ), we find that $\displaystyle\|(v_{0}-\bar{v}(0,\cdot),u_{0}-\bar{u}(0,\cdot),\theta_{0}-\bar{\theta}(0,\cdot))\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}<\varepsilon_{*}+\hat{C}_{1}\sqrt{\delta_{0}}=\frac{\varepsilon}{2(C_{0}+1)}-\delta_{0}^{1/40}-e^{-c\delta_{S}\beta}.$ Then, Proposition 3.2 implies that $\sup_{t\in[0,T_{M}]}\left(\|v-\bar{v}\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+\|u-\bar{u}\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\right)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2},$ which is a contradiction. Therefore, we deduce $T_{M}=+\infty$ , and the a priori estimate ( 3.5 ) holds for the whole time interval $[0,\infty)$ .
